\displaystyle\quad+(2\pi h_{t})^{-D/2}\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\frac{\alpha_{t}x_{0}-\Pi_{k}(x,t)}{h_{t}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0}).

(A.2)

Substituting (
A.1
) and (
A.2
) into
\nabla\log p_{t}=\nabla p_{t}/p_{t}
gives rise to

\displaystyle\nabla\log p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}w_{k}(x,t)\left[-\frac{x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)}{h_{t}}+\frac{\alpha_{t}y_{k}(x,t)-\Pi_{k}(x,t)}{h_{t}}\right],

(A.3)

where

\displaystyle w_{k}(x,t)=

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})}{\sum_{j=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{j}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{j}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\int\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\textrm{data},k}(x_{0})}{\sum_{j=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\textrm{data},j}(x_{0})}

and

\displaystyle y_{k}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}x_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})}{\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}x_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\textrm{data},k}(x_{0})}{\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\textrm{data},k}(x_{0})}.

Moreover, we replace
P_{\textrm{data},k}
by its normalized version
\mu_{k}
defined in Remark
2.14
. This yields

\displaystyle y_{k}(x,t)=\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}x_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{k}(x_{0})}{\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{k}(x_{0})}=\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\mu_{k}}[X_{0}|X_{t}=x].

Substituting
y_{k}(x,t)=\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\mu_{k}}[X_{0}|X_{t}=x]
into (
A.3
) completes the proof.

A.2
Proof of Lemma
3.2

For any fixed time
t>0
and noisy state
x\in\alpha_{t}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{M}_{t},r)
, we represent the score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
as

\displaystyle\nabla\log p_{t}(x)=\frac{\nabla p_{t}(x)}{p_{t}(x)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi h_{t})^{-D/2}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}-\frac{x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}}{h_{t}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})}{(2\pi h_{t})^{-D/2}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\left(-\frac{x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)}{h_{t}}-\frac{\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{0}}{h_{t}}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})}{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)}{h_{t}}+\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\left(\frac{\alpha_{t}x_{0}-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)}{h_{t}}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})}{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{-\frac{x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)}{h_{t}}}_{s_{\perp}:~\text{orthogonal score}}+\underbrace{\frac{\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[X_{0}|X_{t}=x]-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)}{h_{t}}}_{s_{\mathcal{M}}:~\text{on-support score}},

(A.4)

where in the last equality (
A.4
), we invoke

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{0}|X_{t}=x]=\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}x_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})}{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})}.

Furthermore, we decompose
p_{t}
by

\displaystyle p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\frac{1}{(2\pi h_{t})^{D/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi h_{t})^{D/2}}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)+\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi h_{t})^{D/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}+2\langle x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t),\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0}).

Here in the last equality, we expand the squared distance and observe that
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)
is independent of the integrand
x_{0}
. Taking logarithm and gradient with respect to
x
on
p_{t}
gives rise to

\begin{split}\nabla\log p_{t}(x)&=\underbrace{\nabla_{x}\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)}_{(\spadesuit)}+g(x,t),\end{split}

(A.5)

where

\displaystyle g(x,t)=\nabla_{x}\log\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\exp\left(\frac{-\|\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}+2\langle x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t),\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0}).

For term
(\spadesuit)
, using the chain rule of differentiation, we have

\displaystyle(\spadesuit)=\nabla_{x}\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)=-\left(I_{D}-\nabla_{x}\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)\right)\frac{x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)}{h_{t}}.

Note that
\nabla_{x}\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)
is the Jacobian matrix of the projection operator. Invoking the identity
\nabla_{x}\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)(x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t))=0
in Lemma
E.1
, we deduce

\displaystyle(\spadesuit)=-\frac{x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)}{h_{t}}.

Substituting
(\spadesuit)
into (
A.5
), we derive

\displaystyle\nabla\log p_{t}(x)=-\frac{x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)}{h_{t}}+g(x,t).

Comparing with (
A.4
), we conclude that
g(x,t)=s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)
, which completes the proof.

Appendix B
Proofs in Section
4

With the goal of proving Theorem
4.4
, we present the analysis for small noise first in Appendix
B.1
and large noise in Appendix
B.2
. In Appendix
B.3
, we combine the results in large and small noise regimes to complete the proof of Theorem
4.4
. To ease the presentation, we frequently defer technical lemmas to Appendix
D
.

B.1
Small Noise: Proof of Lemma
4.6

The small noise regime is determined by the following conditions on time
t
,

\displaystyle h_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\epsilon^{2/\beta},\frac{\eta\tau}{16\sqrt{D}B},\frac{\min\{\tau,\tau^{2}\}}{\min\{\tau,\tau^{2}\}+256\log(1/\epsilon)\max\{D,16L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}\}/\eta^{2}}\right\},

(B.1)

where
\epsilon\in(0,1)
denotes the desired approximation error and
\eta\in(0,1]
is specified in Assumption
4.2
. The conditions in (
B.1
) identify the small time regime where the variance
h_{t}
of the added noise is smaller than the target accuracy
\epsilon
, while simultaneously ensuring that the projections of input points onto the manifold are well-defined. Notably, in the limit of small approximation error
\epsilon\to 0
, the constraints in (
B.1
) are dominated by the condition
h_{t}\leq\epsilon^{2/\beta}
.
Conditions in (
B.1
) imply the following two inequalities:

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\epsilon)}\leq\frac{\eta\alpha_{t}}{8}\min\{\sqrt{\tau},\tau\}\quad\text{and}\quad\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}\leq\frac{\eta\tau}{16}.

(B.2)

These bounds are used later to ensure that projections from a truncated region onto
\mathcal{M}
are well-defined.

Our goal is to construct a network
\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)
that approximates the score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
in the
L^{2}(P_{t})
norm. For a given time
t>0
, we define the truncated region

\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)=\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}).

Our approach involves a dual treatment of
\bar{s}_{\rm small}
depending on the spatial domain: within the region
\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
, we design
\bar{s}_{\rm small}
to achieve pointwise approximation in terms of the
L_{\infty}
norm, while outside this region, we only require
\bar{s}_{\rm small}
to remain bounded.
The approximation error can then be decomposed as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(\cdot,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}\|_{L^{2}(P_{t})}^{2}&\leq\sup_{x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}\\
&\quad+\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}P_{t}(x)}_{=~\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\delta^{2}/h_{t})\text{ by Lemma~\ref{lemma:lowp-bound}}}.\end{split}

(B.3)

By Lemma
E.2
, we show that
\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
is a high probability region, i.e.,
\mathbb{P}[X_{t}\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)]\geq 1-\delta^{D}
for
\delta\in(0,e^{-2})
. Accordingly, the second error term on the complement of
\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
only contributes a minor error, which scales as
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\delta^{2}/h_{t})
by Lemma
D.1
. In the sequel, we focus on bounding the first error term.

In the small noise regime, the radius of
\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
satisfies
2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}\leq\alpha_{t}\tau
once we set
\delta=\epsilon
in (
B.2
), and thereby the projection of
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
onto
\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M}
is well-defined
[
41
]
. In this case, Lemma
3.2
decomposes the score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
into the on-support score
s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)
and the orthogonal score
s_{\bot}(x,t)
. Our proof focuses primarily on approximating the more complicated on-support score
s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)
. Nonetheless, the orthogonal score
s_{\bot}
can be approximated using the same components developed for
s_{\mathcal{M}}
.

We represent the on-support score
s_{\mathcal{M}}
by writing it as a fraction:

\displaystyle s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)=\frac{s_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{s_{1}(x,t)},

(B.4)

where we denote the projection
x^{*}_{t}=\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)/\alpha_{t}
and define

\displaystyle s_{1}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}+2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})

(B.5)

and

\displaystyle s_{2}(x,t)=\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}(x^{*}_{t}-x_{0})\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}+2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0}).

We approximate
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
separately, and the approximation to
s_{1}
consists of two steps: 1) constructing local polynomials to approximate
s_{1}
(Appendix
B.1.1
), and 2) implementing local polynomials using neural networks (Appendix
B.1.2
). The approximation error of
s_{1}
is provided in Lemma
B.1
(Appendix
B.1.3
). The approximation of
s_{2}
follows a similar procedure and is postponed to Lemma
B.2
(Appendix
B.1.4
).

B.1.1
Local Polynomial Construction

By Remark
2.8
, we equip
\mathcal{M}
with an atlas
\{(U_{k},\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k})\}_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}
, where
U_{k}=\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r))
. We choose the radius
r
to satisfy

\displaystyle r<\min\{3\tau,\eta L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\tau,\eta\tau/(4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})\}.

(B.6)

Here
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}=\max_{k}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}
for
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}>0
being the Lipschitz constant of
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
, and
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}=\max_{k}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}}
for
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}}>0
being the Lipschitz constant of
\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}
. A precise value of the radius
r
will be set later according to the approximation accuracy.
The number of charts in the atlas is bounded in Lemma
D.2
, which reads

C_{\mathcal{M}}\leq\frac{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{d}T_{d}}{r^{d}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\textrm{d}\mu_{\mathcal{M}},

(B.7)

with
T_{d}
the thickness of the charts, defined as the average number of charts that contain a point on
\mathcal{M}

[
16
]
.

Using the decomposition of
P_{\rm data}
in (
2.2
) with respect to the atlas
\{(U_{k},\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k})\}_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}
, we have

\displaystyle s_{1}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}+2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v,

where
F_{k}(v)=\rho_{k}(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v))\cdot p_{\rm data}(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v))\cdot G_{k}(v)
. Now let
\Delta(t)>0
and
\bar{r}(t)>0
be two truncation radii that will be chosen later. Given an arbitrary
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
, we define the low-dimensional representation
v_{k}(x,t)
and the index set
\mathcal{I}(x)
as

v_{k}(x,t)=\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}(x^{*}_{t})=\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}(\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)/\alpha_{t})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{I}(x)=\{k\leq C_{\mathcal{M}}:\|x_{k}-x\|\leq\bar{r}(t)\}.

For any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
and
u,v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we also introduce the shorthands:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,v,t)

\displaystyle=\left\langle x-\alpha_{t}x_{t}^{*},\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\right\rangle\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{k}(u,v)

\displaystyle=\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(u)-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}.

Then we define
f_{1}
as

\displaystyle f_{1}(x,t)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,v,t)\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}\mathcal{D}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v)\right)F_{k}(v)\textrm{d}v.

(B.8)

Here, we substitute
u=v_{k}(x,t)
into
\mathcal{D}_{k}(u,v,t)
. Function
f_{1}
resembles a similar formula to
s_{1}
. Yet, function
f_{1}
only integrates over a small ball of radius
\Delta(t)
. Moreover,
f_{1}
neglects many charts with a center
x_{k}
that is distant from the given noisy state
x
. Thanks to Lemma
D.3
, we show that
f_{1}
can still approximate
s_{1}
as long as
\Delta(t)
and
\bar{r}(t)
are set suitably. Specifically, for any given
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
, we choose

\displaystyle\Delta(t)

\displaystyle=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})\big(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+(d/2)\log(1/h_{t})\big)}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\bar{r}(t)

\displaystyle=rL_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}+2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}+\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\Delta(t).

Then for any
t<t_{\rm small}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}|f_{1}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|=\mathcal{O}(h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{1}).

(B.9)

We remark that function
f_{1}
possesses simplicity and efficiency for developing a neural network approximation, since it involves less charts.

In order to implement
f_{1}
using neural networks, we first construct a polynomial approximation to
f_{1}
, which boils down to constructing polynomial approximation to components in
f_{1}
.

\bullet

1. Local density-related function
F_{k}
.

Recall that
F_{k}(v)=(\rho_{k}\cdot p_{\rm data})\circ\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\cdot G_{k}(v)
for
v\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)
.
We claim that
F_{k}
is a non-negative
\beta
-Hölder function on
\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)
with a bounded Hölder norm
C_{F}>0
. To see this, we observe that
p_{\rm data}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta}(\mathcal{M})
, while the partition of unity
\rho_{k}
, the exponential map
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
, and the square root of the determinant of the Jacobian matrix
G_{k}
are all
C^{\infty}
. It is clear that
C_{F}
depends on the bounds on the derivatives of
\rho_{k}
,
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
, and
G_{k}
. Accordingly, we adopt Taylor polynomials of degree
\lfloor\beta\rfloor
to approximate
F_{k}
. Let
\theta=[\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{d}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
be a multi-index, and we define

\widehat{F}_{k}(v)=\sum_{|{\theta}|\leq\lfloor\beta\rfloor}a_{\theta}^{k}v^{\theta}\quad\text{with}\quad a_{\theta}^{k}=\frac{1}{\theta_{1}!\cdots\theta_{d}!}\partial^{\theta}F_{k}(0).

(B.10)

Invoking Theorem 3 in
11
, we have

\sup_{v\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)}|\widehat{F}_{k}(v)-{F}_{k}(v)|\leq C_{F}d^{\beta}r^{\beta}.

(B.11)

This indicates that
\widehat{F}_{k}(v)
can uniformly approximate
F_{k}(v)
up to an arbitrarily small approximation error as long as the radius
r
is small enough. Therefore, we define
f_{2}
by replacing
F_{k}
in
f_{1}
by
\widehat{F}_{k}
:

\displaystyle f_{2}(x,t)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,v,t)\right)\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}\mathcal{D}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v)\right)\cdot\widehat{F}_{k}(v)\textrm{d}v.

The approximation error of
f_{2}
to
f_{1}
is established in Lemma
D.8
. In particular, for any approximation error
\epsilon_{2}<(\min\{3\tau,\eta L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\tau,\eta\tau/(4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})\})^{\beta}
, we set the radius of charts to be

r=\epsilon_{2}^{1/\beta}.

This choice of
r
validates the condition in (
B.6
). Then for any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
, we have

\displaystyle|f_{2}(x,t)-f_{1}(x,t)|=\mathcal{O}(h_{t}^{d/2}|\mathcal{I}(x)|\epsilon_{2}).

(B.12)

\bullet

2. Exponential function
\exp(\cdot)
.
We approximate the two exponential functions in
f_{2}
by their Taylor series expanded around
0
, i.e.,
\exp(-a)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{j!}a^{j}
for any
a\in\mathbb{R}
. We truncate the degree at
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
, respectively, which gives rise to

\displaystyle f_{3}(x,t)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\left[\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{j!}\frac{({\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,v,t))^{j}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t})^{j}}\right]\cdot\left[\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}l!}\frac{(\mathcal{D}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v))^{l}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}\right]\widehat{F}_{k}(v)\textrm{d}v.

Controlling the approximation error of
f_{3}
to
f_{2}
is relatively simple, as the exponential function is
C^{\infty}
. Specifically, for any given
\epsilon_{3}\in(0,1)
, we set
\gamma^{\prime}={\mathcal{O}}(\log(1/\epsilon_{3})+\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+\log(1/h_{t}))
; recall that
\epsilon_{1}
is the approximation error of
f_{1}
in (
B.9
). Then by Lemma
D.9
, for any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
, it holds that

\displaystyle|f_{3}(x,t)-f_{2}(x,t)|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(h_{t}^{d/2}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma!}\left(\frac{16L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}{\alpha_{t}\tau}\right)^{\gamma}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\right).

(B.13)

We leave
\gamma
unspecified, which will be chosen later depending on the curvature of the manifold.

\bullet

3. Exponential map
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
.
Different from the previous two components, we use average Taylor polynomials to approximate the exponential map
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
and
\mathcal{D}_{k}
. The use of average Taylor polynomials allows us to simultaneously approximate
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
and all its first-order derivatives. For attentive readers, we provide an introduction to average Taylor polynomials in Appendix
F
.

It suffices to approximate each coordinate in
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
. For the
i
-th coordinate map
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}
with
i\in\{1,\ldots,D\}
, we approximate it by an average Taylor polynomial
\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}^{S}
of degree
S
averaged over
\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)
. Let
\theta=[\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{d}]^{\top}\!\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
be a multi-index again. We define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}^{S}(v)\overset{(i)}{=}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)}\left(\sum_{|\theta|<S}\frac{\partial^{\theta}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(z)}{|\theta|!}(v-z)^{\theta}\right)\phi(z)dz\overset{(ii)}{=}\sum_{|\theta|<S}c^{k,i}_{\theta}v^{\theta},

(B.14)

where equality
(i)
follows the averaged Taylor polynomial in Definition
F.1
, with
\phi(z)
an arbitrary cut-off function, and quality
(ii)
implies that the averaged Taylor polynomial can be written as a sum of monomials as established in Lemma
F.3
. Now we instantiate
S=\beta+1
and define

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}}(u,v)=\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}^{\beta+1}(u)-\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}^{\beta+1}(v)

as the coordinate-wise difference. Then we approximate
\mathcal{D}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v)
by

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v)=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\left(\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}}(v_{k}(x,t),v)\right)^{2}.

Lemma
D.10
proves that
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}
well approximates
\mathcal{D}_{k}
with respect to the Sobolev
W^{1,\infty}
norm. Substituting
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}
into
f_{3}
leads to

\displaystyle f_{4}(x,t)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\left[\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{j!}\frac{({\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,v,t))^{j}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t})^{j}}\right]\cdot\left[\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}l!}\frac{(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v))^{l}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}\right]\widehat{F}_{k}(v)\textrm{d}v.

Exploiting the approximation error of
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}
to
\mathcal{D}_{k}
in Lemma
D.10
, we show in Lemma
D.11
that for any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
, it holds that

\displaystyle|f_{4}(x,t)-f_{3}(x,t)|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\big(h_{t}^{d/2}|\mathcal{I}(x)|\epsilon_{2}\big).

(B.15)

Next, we approximate
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,v,t)=\left\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\right\rangle
using
\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}}
. We collect
\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}}
for
i=1,\dots,D
into a
D
-dimensional vector:

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v)=\left[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,1}}(v_{k}(x,t),v),\dots,\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,D}}(v_{k}(x,t),v)\right]^{\top}.

Then
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
is realized by

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{T}}}_{k}(x,v,t)=\left\langle x-\alpha_{t}x_{t}^{*},\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v)\right\rangle.

Note that
f_{4}
involves powers of
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}
. We adopt the tensor notation to write

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{{\mathcal{T}}}_{k}(x,v,t)\right)^{j}=\left\langle[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]^{\otimes j},[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v)]^{\otimes j}\right\rangle.

Substituting
(\widehat{{\mathcal{T}}}_{k})^{j}
into
f_{4}
, we derive

\displaystyle\quad~f_{5}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\left[\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{j!}\frac{(\widehat{{\mathcal{T}}}_{k}(x,v,t))^{j}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t})^{j}}\right]\cdot\left[\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}l!}\frac{(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v))^{l}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}\right]\widehat{F}_{k}(v)\textrm{d}v

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{j!(h_{t})^{j/2}}\left\langle[x-\alpha_{t}x_{t}^{*}]^{\otimes j},\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}(v_{k}(x,t),h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right\rangle,

where

\displaystyle\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}(v_{k}(x,t),h_{t},\alpha_{t})

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\frac{[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v)]^{\otimes j}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{j/2}}\cdot\left[\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}l!}\frac{(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v))^{l}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}\right]\widehat{F}_{k}(v)\textrm{d}v.

Expressing the chart determination via indicator functions and rearranging terms yields

\displaystyle f_{5}(x,t)=\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{1}{j!}\left\langle\frac{(-1)^{j}}{h_{t}^{j/2}}[x-\alpha_{t}x_{t}^{*}]^{\otimes j},\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\ind(k\in\mathcal{I}(x))\cdot\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}(v_{k}(x,t),h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right\rangle.

(B.16)

Notably, the integration in
\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}
is performed over polynomials within a Euclidean ball centered at
v_{k}(x,t)
, thus
\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}
is still a polynomial with respect to
v_{k}(x,t)
.
By Lemma
D.13
, we establish the approximation error: for any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
, we have

\displaystyle|f_{5}(x,t)-f_{4}(x,t)|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(D^{\gamma/2}h_{t}^{d/2}\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\epsilon_{2}\right).

(B.17)

Finally, we can conclude the result in Lemma
4.7
by summing up the approximation errors in (
B.9
), (
B.12
), (
B.13
), (
B.15
) and (
B.17
). Letting
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{2}=\epsilon_{3}=\delta=\epsilon
, for any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle|f_{5}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{\gamma/2}h_{t}^{d/2}|\mathcal{I}(x)|\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\gamma!}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}{\alpha_{t}\tau}\right)^{\gamma}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right),

(B.18)

where
\lesssim
hides the dependence on
d,\beta,T_{d},C_{F}
, the upper bounds on the derivatives of exponential maps, and logarithmic factors.
Applying Lemma
D.2
on the region
\{z\in\mathcal{M}:\|z-x\|\leq\bar{r}(t)+r\}
, we can bound the number of selected charts
|\mathcal{I}(x)|
by

|\mathcal{I}(x)|\leq\frac{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{d}T_{d}}{r^{d}}\int_{z\in\mathcal{M}}\ind(\|z-x\|\leq\bar{r}(t)+r)\textrm{d}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(z).

Recall our choice of
\bar{r}(t)
:

\displaystyle\bar{r}(t)=L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}r+2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\epsilon)}+\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}+2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon+d\log(1/h_{t})/2))}.

Under the condition in (
B.1
) that
h_{t}\leq\epsilon^{2/\beta}=r^{2}
, we have
\bar{r}(t)\lesssim\sqrt{D}r
, where
\lesssim
hides logarithmic factors and dependence on
d
,
B
and the upper bounds on the derivatives of exponential maps. This further yields

|\mathcal{I}(x)|\lesssim\frac{\left(\bar{r}(t)+r\right)^{d}}{r^{d}}\lesssim D^{d/2}.

Therefore, plugging
|\mathcal{I}(x)|
in (
B.1.1
) yields

\displaystyle|f_{5}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{(\gamma+d)/2}h_{t}^{d/2}\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\left(\frac{(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}}{\tau^{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right).

B.1.2
Neural Network Implementation

We construct our neural network based on implementing function
f_{5}
in (
B.16
). We start with developing two elementary networks: 1)
{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}
approximates the low-dimensional representation
v_{k}
, and 2)
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\times}
realizes multiplication. We then construct three key networks: 1) projection network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm proj}
, 2) chart determination network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm chart}
, and 3) Tensor-Poly network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm poly}
to approximate key components in
f_{5}
.
Accordingly, the constructed network takes the form:

\displaystyle\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)=\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{1}{j!}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\times}^{j}\left({\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm proj}^{j}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t}),\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\times}^{1}\left({\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k}),{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}^{k,j}({\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}(x,t),h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right)\right).

(B.19)

In the sequel, we present a detailed construction of the two elementary networks followed by the three key networks.

Elementary Network Construction
. We first approximate the low-dimensional representation
v_{k}(x,t)
of a noisy state
x
at time
t
. By definition,

v_{k}(x,t)
projects
x
onto the shrunk manifold
\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M}
first, then maps the projection to the low-dimensional space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Thanks to Proposition
4.8
,
v_{k}
is
C^{\infty}
in
x
and thereby can be efficiently approximated by a network. By Lemma
D.14
, for any approximation error
\epsilon_{v}\in(0,1)
, there exists a network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}=[{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},1},{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},2},\ldots,{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},d}]^{\top}
satisfying

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}\left\|{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}(x,t)-v_{k}(x,t)\right\|\leq\epsilon_{v},

(B.20)

with each coordinate map
{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},i}\in\mathcal{F}(L_{v},W_{v},S_{v},B_{v},\cdot)
for

L_{v}=\mathcal{O}(\log(1/\epsilon_{v})),~~W_{v}=\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon_{v}^{-d/\beta}\log(1/\epsilon_{v})\right),~~S_{v}=\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon_{v}^{-d/\beta}\log(1/\epsilon_{v})\right),~~B_{v}=\mathcal{O}(\max\{B_{\mathcal{M}},\tau^{2}\}).

We then implement the multiplication operation. Invoking Lemma
D.17
, we construct a network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\times}^{j}\in\mathcal{F}(L_{\times},W_{\times},S_{\times},B_{\times},\cdot)
that realizes the
j
-th order tensor inner product, i.e., given
\epsilon_{\times}\in(0,1)
, for any
u_{1},u_{2}\in[-2\sqrt{D\log(1/\epsilon)},2\sqrt{D\log(1/\epsilon)}]^{D}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathfrak{N}}_{\times}^{j}\left(u_{1}^{\otimes j},u_{2}^{\otimes j}\right)-\left\langle u_{1}^{\otimes j},u_{2}^{\otimes j}\right\rangle\right|\leq D^{j}\epsilon_{\times}.

(B.21)

The corresponding network configuration is

\displaystyle L_{\times}=\mathcal{O}(\log(D/\epsilon_{\times})),\quad W_{\times}=\mathcal{O}(D^{j}),\quad S_{\times}=\mathcal{O}(D^{j}\log(D/\epsilon_{\times})),\quad\text{and}\quad B_{\times}=\mathcal{O}(D^{2}\log(1/\epsilon)).

Key Networks Construction
. It remains to construct three key networks. We begin with the projection network.

\bullet

1. Projection network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm proj}
.
We construct a network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm proj}^{j}
that implements each entry in the tensor
[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]^{\otimes j}
. As a function of input
x
, the tensor
[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]^{\otimes j}
is
C^{\infty}
for fixed time
t
, since the projection
\alpha_{t}x_{t}^{*}=\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)
is
C^{\infty}
for
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
due to
Proposition
4.8
and restrictions to the small noise regime (
B.2
). Therefore, each entry of
[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]^{\otimes j}
can be approximated by a network as follows.
Lemma
D.18
establishes approximation guarantees for
[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]^{\otimes j}
. Given an order
j
and
\epsilon_{\rm proj}\in(0,1)
, there exists
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm proj}^{j}
such that for any time
t\in[t_{0},t_{\rm small}]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left\|{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm proj}^{j}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-\frac{[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]^{\otimes j}}{h_{t}^{j/2}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj}.

(B.22)

Here
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm proj}^{j}
is a network in
\mathcal{F}(L_{\rm proj},W_{\rm proj},S_{\rm proj},B_{\rm proj},\cdot)
, where

\displaystyle L_{\rm proj}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}\big(1/(h_{t_{0}}^{j/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj})\big)\right),\quad W_{\rm proj}=\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon_{\rm proj}^{-d/\beta}\log\big(1/(h_{t_{0}}^{j/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj})\big)+\log^{4}(1/h_{t_{0}})\right),

\displaystyle S_{\rm proj}=\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon_{\rm proj}^{-d/\beta}\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}^{j/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj}))+\log^{4}(1/h_{t_{0}})\right),\quad\text{and}\quad B=h_{t_{0}}^{-j}.

\bullet

2. Chart determination network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}
. The chart determination network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}
identifies the index set
\mathcal{I}(x)=\{k:\|x-x_{k}\|\leq\bar{r}(t)\}
. For an index
k\in\mathcal{I}(x)
, it must verify the condition
\mathds{1}\{\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}\leq\bar{r}(t)^{2}\}=1
. As a result,
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm det}
implements the indicator function by (i) approximating the squared Euclidean distance using a network
\widehat{d}
, and (ii) implementing the indicator function using another network
\widehat{\mathds{1}}
. The composition
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm det}=\widehat{\mathds{1}}\circ\widehat{d}
yields the desired approximation to the index set.
The detailed network construction and approximation guarantee are provided in Lemma
D.19
. Given
\epsilon_{\rm{det}}\in(0,1)
,
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, and any
k\in[C_{\mathcal{M}}]
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})=\begin{cases}1&\text{if}~\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}\leq(1-\epsilon_{\rm det})\bar{r}(t)^{2}\\
0&\text{if}~\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}\geq\bar{r}(t)^{2},\end{cases}

(B.23)

Here
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}
is a network in
\mathcal{F}(L_{\rm{det}},W_{\rm{det}},S_{\rm{det}},B_{\rm{det}},\cdot)
, where

\displaystyle L_{\rm{det}}=\mathcal{O}(\log(D/\epsilon)+\log(1/\epsilon_{\rm{det}})),\quad W_{\rm{det}}=\mathcal{O}(D),

\displaystyle S_{\rm{det}}=\mathcal{O}(\log(D/\epsilon)+\log(1/\epsilon_{\rm{det}})),\quad\text{and}\quad B_{\rm{det}}=\mathcal{O}(\log(D/\epsilon)+\log(1/\epsilon_{\rm{det}})).

\bullet

3. Tensor-Poly network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}
. We implement the entries of
\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}
in (
B.16
) by a single network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}^{k,j}
. Since each entry of
\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}
is a polynomial with a
d
-dimensional input and degree at most
(\beta+1)(j+\gamma^{\prime}+1)
, where we set
\gamma^{\prime}={\mathcal{O}}\left(\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right)
, it can be efficiently approximated by a neural network. By Lemma
D.20
, given
\epsilon_{\rm{poly}}\in(0,1)
, there exists a network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}^{k,j}
such that for any
t\in[t_{0},t_{\rm small}]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)}\Big\|{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}^{k,j}(v,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}(v,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\Big\|_{\infty}\leq h_{t_{0}}^{d/2}\epsilon_{\rm{poly}}.

(B.24)

Here each entry of
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}^{k,j}
is a network in
\mathcal{F}(L_{\rm poly},W_{\rm poly},S_{\rm poly},B_{\rm poly},\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L_{\rm poly}=\mathcal{O}(\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon_{\rm{poly}})),\quad W_{\rm poly}=\mathcal{O}\left((\gamma+\gamma^{\prime}+1)^{d}\right),

\displaystyle S_{\rm poly}=\mathcal{O}\left((\gamma+\gamma^{\prime}+1)^{d}\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon_{\rm{poly}}))\right),~~\text{and}~~B_{\rm poly}=\mathcal{O}(1)

B.1.3
Bounding Approximation Error of
\bar{s}_{1}

We state the approximation guarantee of the network implementation
\bar{s}_{1}
in the following lemma.

Lemma B.1

(Approximation error of
s_{1}(x,t)
)
.

Given
\epsilon\in\big(0,(\min\{1,3\tau,\eta L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\tau,\eta\tau/(4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})\})^{\beta}\big)
and time
t\geq t_{0}
satisfying (
B.1
), let
r=\epsilon^{1/\beta}
.
Then for any fixed
\gamma>0
, there exists a network
\bar{s}_{1}\in\mathcal{F}(L_{1},W_{1},S_{1},B_{1},\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),\quad W_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(D^{\gamma}\vee\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),

\displaystyle S_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\left(\gamma\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))+\log^{4}(1/h_{t_{0}})\right)+\gamma D^{\gamma}\log(1/\epsilon)\right),\quad B_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(h_{t_{0}}^{-\gamma}\right),

such that for all
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle|\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|\lesssim D^{\gamma+d/2}h_{t}^{d/2}\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\left(\frac{(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}}{\tau^{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right).

Here
\lesssim
hides logarithmic factors and dependence on
d
,
\beta
,
B
,
C_{F}
and the upper bounds on the derivatives of exponential maps.

Proof of Lemma
B.1
.

First, by Lemma
4.7
,
s_{1}
can be approximated by
f_{5}
in (
B.16
) with the following approximation error

\displaystyle|f_{5}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{(\gamma+d)/2}h_{t}^{d/2}\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\left(\frac{(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}}{\tau^{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right),

which holds for all
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
.
Replacing the low-dimensional representation
v_{k}(x,t)
by its network implementation
{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}
in
f_{5}(x,t)
(
B.16
) gives rise to

\displaystyle f_{6}(x,t)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{j!(h_{t})^{j/2}}\left\langle[x-\alpha_{t}x_{t}^{*}]^{\otimes j},\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}({\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}(x,t),h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right\rangle.

(B.25)

Lemma
D.15
bounds the error of
f_{6}
approximating
f_{5}
:

\displaystyle|f_{6}(x,t)-f_{5}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{\gamma+d/2}h_{t}^{d/2}(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}\epsilon_{v}.

This error is controlled by the approximation cost
\epsilon_{v}
given in (
B.20
), together with the Lipschitz property of
f_{5}(x,t)
with respect to
v_{k}(x,t)
(Lemma
D.16
). Now we combine the results in (
B.20
)-(
B.24
). Taking
\epsilon_{v}=\epsilon_{\text{proj}}=\epsilon_{\rm det}=\epsilon_{\text{poly}}=\epsilon
and
\epsilon_{\times}=h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon
, we have

\displaystyle|\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)-f_{6}(x,t)|\lesssim h_{t}^{d/2}D^{\gamma}\left(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2\right)^{\gamma}\epsilon+D\gamma h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon.

Here
\bar{s}_{1}
defined in (
B.19
) can be written as a feedforward network
\bar{s}_{1}\in\mathcal{F}(L_{1},W_{1},S_{1},B_{1},\cdot)
, where

\displaystyle L_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),\quad W_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(D^{\gamma}\vee\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),

\displaystyle S_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\left(\gamma\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))+\log^{4}(1/h_{t_{0}})\right)+\gamma D^{\gamma}\log(1/\epsilon)\right),\quad B_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(h_{t_{0}}^{-\gamma}\right).

Here
\mathcal{O}(\cdot)
hides dependence on
d
,
\beta
,
B
,
C_{F}
, and the upper bounds on the derivatives of exponential maps.
Therefore, using the triangle inequality, we finally obtain

\displaystyle|\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|\lesssim D^{\gamma+d/2}h_{t}^{d/2}\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\left(\frac{(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}}{\tau^{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right).

The proof is complete.
∎

B.1.4
Bounding Approximation Error of
\bar{s}_{2}

It remains to construct a network
\bar{s}_{2}=\{\bar{s}_{2,i}\}_{i=1}^{D}
that approximates each entry of
s_{2}
:

\displaystyle s_{2}(x,t)=\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}(x^{*}_{t}-x_{0})\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}+2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0}).

The construction follow the same approximation procedure as that to
s_{1}
. We provide the approximation guarantees as follows.

Lemma B.2

(Approximation result for
s_{2}(x,t)
)
.

Given
\epsilon\in\big(0,(\min\{1,3\tau,\eta L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\tau,\eta\tau/(4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})\})^{\beta}\big)
, and time
t\geq t_{0}
satisfying (
B.1
), let
r=\epsilon^{1/\beta}
.
Then for any fixed
\gamma>0
, there exists a network
\bar{s}_{2}=\{\bar{s}_{2,i}\}_{i=1}^{D}
such that for all
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)-s_{2}(x,t)\|\lesssim D^{\gamma+(d+1)/2}h_{t}^{d/2}\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\left(\frac{(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}}{\tau^{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right).

Here each entry
\bar{s}_{2,i}
is a network in
\mathcal{F}(L_{2},W_{2},S_{2},B_{2},\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),\quad W_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(D^{\gamma}\vee\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),

\displaystyle S_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\left(\gamma\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))+\log^{4}(1/h_{t_{0}})\right)+\gamma D^{\gamma}\log(1/\epsilon)\right)\quad B_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(h_{t_{0}}^{-\gamma}\right).

Proof of Lemma
B.2
.

Compared to
s_{1}(x,t)
, the main difference in
s_{2}(x,t)
is the extra
-\alpha_{t}(x^{*}_{t}-x_{0})/\sqrt{h_{t}}
in the integrand. It suffices to slightly enlarge the truncation radius to to cancel out the scale of this extra term,

\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon)+(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})/2)},

then the analysis in Lemma
B.1
can be carried through. Consequently, the degrees of the polynomial approximators with respect to each entry of
s_{2}
are increased by
1
, while this does not affect the order of approximation error and network size. Therefore, for
i=1,\ldots,D
and any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle|\bar{s}_{2,i}(x,t)-s_{2,i}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{\gamma+d/2}h_{t}^{d/2}\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\left(\frac{(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}}{\tau^{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right).

The network configuration of
\bar{s}_{2,i}
is in the same order as
\bar{s}_{1}
given in Lemma
B.1
. Finally, we can conclude the proof by applying
\|\bar{s}_{2,i}(x,t)-s_{2,i}(x,t)\|\leq\sqrt{D}\max_{i=1,\ldots,D}|\bar{s}_{2,i}(x,t)-s_{2,i}(x,t)|.

∎

B.1.5
Constructing Network Approximation to
s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)
and
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)

In this step, we construct a neural network to approximate the on-manifold score
s_{\mathcal{M}}
in (
B.4
), using networks
\bar{s}_{1}
(Lemma
B.1
) and
\bar{s}_{2}
(Lemma
B.2
).
The formal result is summarized as follows.

Lemma B.3
.

Given
\epsilon\in\big(0,(\min\{1,3\tau,\eta L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\tau,\eta\tau/(4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})\})^{\beta}\big)
and time
t\geq t_{0}
satisfying (
B.1
), let
r=\epsilon^{1/\beta}
. Then there exists a network
\bar{s}_{\mathcal{M}}\in\mathcal{F}(L,W,S,B,\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L=\mathcal{O}\left(\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),\quad W=\mathcal{O}\left(D^{\gamma}\vee\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),

\displaystyle S=\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\left(\gamma\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))+\log^{4}(1/h_{t_{0}})\right)+\gamma D^{\gamma}\log(1/\epsilon)\right),\quad B=\mathcal{O}\left(h_{t_{0}}^{-\gamma}\right),

such that for all
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{D^{\gamma+d/2+1}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\exp\left(\frac{5(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\left(\frac{(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}}{\tau^{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right).

Here
\lesssim
hides dependence on
d,\beta,B,C_{f}
and the upper bounds on the derivatives of exponential maps, and
\gamma>0
is some constant to be chosen.

Proof of Lemma
B.3
.

The idea is to first probe the approximation ability of the ratio

\displaystyle\bar{s}_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)=\frac{\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}

(B.26)

to the targeted score
s_{\mathcal{M}}
. Then we implement (
B.26
) using a neural network by realizing the division operation.

We invoke Lemma
D.21
with approximation errors to
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
given in Lemma
B.1
and
B.2
, respectively. This yields the following error bound:

\displaystyle\left\|s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\frac{\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}\right\|\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{D^{\gamma+d/2+1}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\exp\left(\frac{5(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\left(\frac{(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}}{\tau^{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right).

Then by Lemma F.7 in
52
, there exists a feedforward network
\bar{\phi}
with no more than
\mathcal{O}(\log^{2}(1/\epsilon))
layers, width bounded by
\mathcal{O}(\log^{3}(1/\epsilon))
, at most non-zero
\mathcal{O}(\log^{4}(1/\epsilon))
neurons and weight parameters bounded by
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-2})
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{\phi}\left(\bar{s}_{1}(x,t),\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)\right)-\frac{\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}\right\|\leq\epsilon.

Taking
\bar{s}_{\mathcal{M}}=\bar{\phi}(\bar{s}_{1},\bar{s}_{2})
leads to

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bar{\phi}\left(\bar{s}_{1}(x,t),\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)\right)-\frac{\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}-s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{D^{\gamma+d/2+1}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\exp\left(\frac{5(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\left(\frac{(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}}{\tau^{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right).

Combined with the network size of
\bar{s}_{1},\bar{s}_{2}
given in Lemma
B.1
and
B.2
, we deduce that

\bar{s}_{\mathcal{M}}\in\mathcal{F}(L,W,S,B,\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L=\mathcal{O}\left(\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),\quad W=\mathcal{O}\left(D^{\gamma}\vee\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),

\displaystyle S=\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\left(\gamma\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))+\log^{4}(1/h_{t_{0}})\right)+\gamma D^{\gamma}\log(1/\epsilon)\right),\quad\text{and}\quad B=\mathcal{O}\left(h_{t_{0}}^{-\gamma}\right).

The proof is complete.
∎

To this end, we are ready to prove the score approximation theory for the small noise regime (Lemma
4.6
). Recall the score decomposition in Lemma
3.2
:

\displaystyle\nabla\log p_{t}(x)=s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\frac{x-\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)}{h_{t}}.

Adopting
\bar{s}_{\mathcal{M}}
given in Lemma
B.3
and the projection network given in Lemma
D.18
, we can construct a network
\bar{s}_{\rm small}\in\mathcal{F}(L,W,S,B,\cdot)
, where

\displaystyle L=\mathcal{O}\left(\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),\quad W=\mathcal{O}\left(D^{\gamma}\vee\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\gamma^{3}\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))\right),

\displaystyle S=\mathcal{O}\left(\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\left(\gamma\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon))+\log^{4}(1/h_{t_{0}})\right)+\gamma D^{\gamma}\log(1/\epsilon)\right),\quad B=\mathcal{O}\left(h_{t_{0}}^{-\gamma}\right).

such that for any
t\geq t_{0}
satisfying (
B.1
) and
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
,

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{D^{\gamma+d/2+1}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\exp\left(\frac{5(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\left(\frac{(\log(1/\epsilon)+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)^{2\gamma}}{\tau^{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\gamma/\beta}+\epsilon\right),

The remaining part repeats the argument in the beginning of Appendix
B.1
with more details.
Recall that we decompose the
L^{2}
approximation error of
\bar{s}_{\rm small}
as as

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{t})}=\left(\int_{x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}+\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\right)\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x.

We can bound the integral within
\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
via the approximation error of
\bar{s}_{\rm small}
:

\displaystyle\int_{x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{D^{2\gamma+d+2}}{h_{t}}\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2\gamma/\beta}}{\tau^{2\gamma}}+\epsilon^{2}\right).

For the integral over
\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left(\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}\right)p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x.

By Lemma
E.2
, we
can bound the probability mass outside the truncation region
\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, which yields,

\displaystyle\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x\leq\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{P}\left(x\notin\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)\right)\leq\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\epsilon^{D}.

Since
\|\nabla p_{t}(x)\|=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)/h_{t}})
for
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, it suffices to clip
\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)
by
R=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)/h_{t}})
via a feedforward layer as constructed in Lemma F.5 of
52
.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x\leq\frac{1}{h_{t}}\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\frac{2\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|^{2}+2DB^{2}}{h_{t}}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x\lesssim\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{h_{t}},

(B.27)

for sufficiently large
D>0
. Finally, we can conclude that the network
\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)
has the following
L^{2}
approximation error for any
t\in[t_{0},t_{\rm small}]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{t})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{D^{2\gamma+d+2}}{h_{t}}\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2\gamma/\beta}}{\tau^{2\gamma}}+\epsilon^{2}\right).

B.2
Large Noise: Proof of Lemma
4.5

Score approximation is less challenging in the large noise regime, where clean data is corrupted by Gaussian noise with large variance
h_{t}
. The resulting score function is smoother than the small noise counterpart.
The large noise regime is characterized by

\displaystyle h_{t}\geq\epsilon^{2/\beta}/4,

(B.28)

where
\epsilon>0
is the approximation error we aim to achieve. We will choose
\epsilon
later so that the large noise and small noise regimes overlap. Our goal is to construct a network
\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)
approximating the score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
in this regime. By the same truncation argument in (
B.3
), it suffices to construct
\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)
for a pointwise approximation to
\nabla\log p_{t}
for any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
.

In this regime, the precise projection
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)
is no longer necessary. Instead, it suffices to decompose the score into components with respect to each local tangent space of
\mathcal{M}
and orthogonal components perpendicular to these tangent spaces. The tangent space projections
\Pi_{k}
have explicit forms of linear operations and thus avoid the complex approximation of
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}
. Lemma
3.1
provides the following score decomposition:

\displaystyle\nabla\log p_{t}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}w_{k}(x,t)\bigg(\frac{\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\mu_{k}}[X_{0}|X_{t}=x]-\Pi_{k}(x,t)}{h_{t}}-\frac{x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)}{h_{t}}\bigg),

where

\displaystyle w_{k}(x,t)=\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})}{\sum_{j=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{j}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{j}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})}

We firstly focus on the approximation of the denominator in the weighting function
w_{k}(x,t)
. For notational simplcity, we denote

\displaystyle s_{3}(x,t):=\sum_{j=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{j}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{j}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0}).

(B.29)

The approximation of
s_{3}
follows a similar process to Section
B.1
: we first approximate the components of
s_{3}
by local polynomials, then implement the local polynomials by neural networks. We defer technical lemmas to Appendix
D.4
. For the numerator, we can take a more direct approach to merge the on-support and orthogonal components together (see details in Section
B.2.3
).

B.2.1
Local Polynomial Construction

Recall that we construct the atlas as
\{(U_{k},\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k})\}_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}
, where
U_{k}=\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r))
. For the target approximation error
\epsilon>0
, we choose the radius
r=\epsilon^{1/\beta}
, which matches the choice in the small noise regime.
We use the same notation
\bar{r}(t)
for the chart determination threshold, but set
\bar{r}(t)=2\sqrt{h_{t}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+h_{t}B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon
, where
\epsilon_{0}>0
is some hyperparameter that we will instantiate later. We define
g_{1}
as

\displaystyle g_{1}(x,t)=\sum_{k:\|x-x_{k}\|\leq\bar{r}(t)}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0}).

Lemma
D.24
demonstrates that
g_{1}(x,t)
well approximates
s_{3}(x,t)
while involves much less charts. For any time
t
satisfying (
B.28
), it holds that

\displaystyle\left\|g_{1}(\cdot,t)-s_{3}(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\epsilon_{0}.

(B.30)

Recall we denote the projection onto the
k
-th tangent space as
\Pi_{k}(x,t):=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{y\in\alpha_{t}\cdot T_{x_{k}}\mathcal{M}}\|y-x\|
, which inspires the following decomposition,

\displaystyle\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}=\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}+{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)+\alpha_{t}^{2}\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t),

where we reload the notations from Section
B.1
as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)

\displaystyle=\langle x-\Pi_{k}(x,t),\Pi_{k}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)

\displaystyle=\|\Pi_{k}(x,t)/\alpha_{t}-x_{0}\|^{2}.

Utilizing the decomposition, we can rewrite
g_{1}
as

\displaystyle g_{1}(x,t)=\sum_{k:\|x-x_{k}\|\leq\bar{r}(t)}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)I_{k}(x,t),

where we define

\displaystyle I_{k}(x,t):=\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0}).

(B.31)

Next, we replace the exponential functions in
I_{k}
by polynomials, which yields

\displaystyle\text{Poly}^{k}(x,t):=

\displaystyle\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma_{0}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{S-1}\frac{(-1)^{l+j}\alpha_{t}^{l+2j}}{2^{j}h_{t}^{l+j}l!j!}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{l}(x,x_{0},t)\mathcal{D}_{k}^{j}(x,x_{0},t)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0}).

(B.32)

The degree
\gamma_{0}>0
will be instantiated later. Lemma
D.25
establishes the approximation error of
\text{Poly}^{k}
to
I_{k}
with
S=4e^{2}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})+4e^{2}(B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}+1)^{2}
. For any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\text{Poly}^{k}(x,t)-I_{k}(x,t)\right|=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+B)^{\gamma_{0}}}{\tau^{\gamma_{0}}}\epsilon^{(d+\gamma_{0})/\beta}+\epsilon^{d/\beta}\epsilon_{0}\right).

(B.33)

Lemma
D.26
further shows that
\text{Poly}^{k}
is a polynomial with inputs
h_{t}
,
\alpha_{t}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
and
P_{k}^{\top}x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where
P_{k}
is a matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{D\times d}
. In addition,
\text{Poly}^{k}
has a degree up to
\gamma_{0}
with respect to
x
and degree up to
S
with respect to
P_{k}^{\top}x
. For notational clarity, we write
\text{Poly}^{k}(x,t)
as
\text{Poly}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})
.
Substituting
\text{Poly}^{k}
for
I_{k}
in
g_{1}
and expressing the chart determination via indicator functions yields

\displaystyle g_{2}(x,t)=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\ind(\|x-x_{k}\|\leq\bar{r}(t))\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\text{Poly}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t}).

(B.34)

B.2.2
Neural Network Implementation

We construct neural networks to implement function
g_{2}
in (
B.34
). The construction involves three key networks: (1) chart determination network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm det}
, (2) polynomial network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm poly}
and (3) weighting network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm exp}
. We also utilize the elementary network
\mathfrak{N}_{\times}
used in Section
B.1.2
to realize multiplication. Accordingly, the constructed network takes the form:

\displaystyle\bar{s}_{3}(x;t)=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\mathfrak{N}_{\times}\left({\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k}),\mathfrak{N}_{\times}\left({\mathfrak{N}}^{k}_{\rm exp}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t}),{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm poly}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right)\right).

(B.35)

In the sequel, we provide detailed implementations of the three key networks:

\bullet

1. Chart determination network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}
. We implement
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})
to approximate
\ind(\|x-x_{k}\|\leq\bar{r}(t))
, following the same construction as the chart determination network described in Section
B.1.2
but using a different
\bar{r}(t)
. By Lemma
D.19
, for
\epsilon_{\rm det}\in(0,1)
,
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, and any
k\in[C_{\mathcal{M}}]
,
we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})=\begin{cases}1&\text{if}~\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}\leq(1-\epsilon_{\rm det})\bar{r}(t)^{2}\\
0&\text{if}~\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}\geq\bar{r}(t)^{2},\end{cases}

Here
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}
is a network in
\mathcal{F}(L_{\rm{det}},W_{\rm{det}},S_{\rm{det}},B_{\rm{det}},\cdot)
, where

\displaystyle L_{\rm{det}}=\mathcal{O}(\log(D/\epsilon)+\log(1/\epsilon_{\rm{det}})),\quad W_{\rm{det}}=\mathcal{O}(D),

\displaystyle S_{\rm{det}}=\mathcal{O}(\log(D/\epsilon)+\log(1/\epsilon_{\rm{det}})),\quad\text{and}\quad B_{\rm{det}}=\mathcal{O}(\log(D/\epsilon)+\log(1/\epsilon_{\rm{det}})).

\bullet

2. Polynomial network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}
.
We proceed to implement
\text{Poly}^{k}
by a network. Applying Theorem 3 in
11
and Lemmas F.6 and F.7 in
52
, for
\epsilon_{\rm poly}\in(0,1)
and each
k=1,\ldots,C_{\mathcal{M}}
, there exists a network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}^{k}
such

\displaystyle\left|{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-\text{Poly}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right|\leq\epsilon_{\rm poly},

which holds for all
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
and
h_{t},\alpha_{t}\in[\epsilon_{\rm poly},1]
.
Here
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}^{k}
is a network in
\mathcal{F}(L_{\rm{poly}},W_{\rm{poly}},S_{\rm{poly}},B_{\rm{poly}},\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L_{\rm{poly}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}(1/\epsilon_{\rm poly})\right),\quad W_{\rm{poly}}=\mathcal{O}\left(D^{\gamma_{0}}S^{d}\log^{3}(1/\epsilon_{\rm poly})\right),

\displaystyle S_{\rm{poly}}=\mathcal{O}\left(D^{\gamma_{0}}S^{d}\log^{4}(1/\epsilon_{\rm poly})\right),\quad\text{and}\quad B_{\rm{poly}}=\mathcal{O}\left(h_{t}^{-(S+\gamma_{0})}\right).

\bullet

3. Network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm exp}
.
It remains to implement
\exp(-\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}/(2h_{t}))
in (
B.34
) by networks. By Lemma F.6, F.7 and F.12 in
52
, for
\epsilon_{\rm exp}\in(0,1)
, there exists a network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm exp}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})
such that for any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
,

\displaystyle\left|{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm exp}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\right|\leq\epsilon_{\rm exp}.

where
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm exp}^{k}
has (i) no more than
c_{\rm exp}\log^{2}(1/\epsilon_{\rm exp})
layers with width bounded by
c_{\rm exp}\log^{3}(1/\epsilon_{\rm exp})
, and (ii) at most
c_{\rm exp}\log^{4}(1/\epsilon_{\rm exp})
neurons and weight parameters, where the constant
c_{\rm exp}
depends on
B
and
D
at most polynomially.

x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
and
h_{t},\alpha_{t}\in[\epsilon_{\rm poly},1]
.
Here
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{exp}}^{k}
is a network in
\mathcal{F}(L_{\rm{exp}},W_{\rm{exp}},S_{\rm{exp}},B_{\rm{exp}},\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L_{\rm{exp}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}(1/\epsilon_{\rm exp})\right),\quad W_{\rm{exp}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}(1/\epsilon_{\rm exp})\right),

\displaystyle S_{\rm{exp}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{4}(1/\epsilon_{\rm exp})\right),\quad\text{and}\quad B_{\rm{exp}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log(1/\epsilon)\right).

The next lemma shows that
s_{3}(x,t)
defined in (
B.29
) can be well approximated by a network.

Lemma B.4
.

Given
\epsilon,\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
and
\gamma_{0}>0
, there exists a network
\bar{s}_{3}\in\mathcal{F}(L_{3},W_{3},S_{3},B_{3},\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L_{3}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(1),\quad W_{3}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(D^{\gamma_{0}}\epsilon^{-d/\beta}),\quad S_{3}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(D^{\gamma_{0}}\epsilon^{-d/\beta}),\quad B_{3}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-2(\log(1/\epsilon_{0})+\gamma_{0})/\beta}),

such that for any time
t>0
satisfying
h_{t}\geq\epsilon^{2/\beta}/4
and
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
,

\displaystyle\left|\bar{s}_{3}(x;t)-s_{3}(x,t)\right|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+B)^{\gamma_{0}}}{\tau^{\gamma_{0}}}h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon^{\gamma_{0}/\beta}+\epsilon_{0}\right).

Proof of Lemma
B.4
.

Recall that
s_{3}(x,t)
is a summation over all the charts, i.e.,

\displaystyle s_{3}(x,t)=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0}).

Notably, (
B.30
) suggests that it suffices to focus on the charts satisfying
\|x-x_{k}\|\leq\bar{r}(t)
. We implement the chart determination by network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}
. According to Lemma
D.19
, we have

\displaystyle\quad~\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})-s_{3}(x,t)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{k:(1-\epsilon_{\rm{det}})\bar{r}(t)<\|x_{k}-x\|<\bar{r}(t)}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})\right|.

Adopting the analysis in Lemma
D.24
, we can derive
\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|\geq(2-4\epsilon_{\rm{det}})\sqrt{h_{t}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}

for
k
-th chart satisfying
\|x_{k}-x\|>(1-\epsilon_{\rm{det}})\bar{r}(t)
and
x_{0}\in U_{k}
. Taking
\epsilon_{\rm{det}}=(2-\sqrt{2})/4
yields

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})-s_{3}(x,t)\bigg|\leq 2\epsilon_{0}.

By the definition of
I_{k}
in (
B.31
), we can rewrite the above inequality as

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)I_{k}(x,t)-s_{3}(x,t)\bigg|\leq 2\epsilon_{0}.

(B.36)

Moreover, we apply
h_{t}\geq\epsilon^{2/\beta}
to (
B.33
), which yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\quad~\left|\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{k}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\text{Poly}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-I_{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right|\\
&=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+B)^{\gamma_{0}}}{\tau^{\gamma_{0}}}h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon^{\gamma_{0}/\beta}+\epsilon_{0}\right).\end{split}

(B.37)

Next, Lemma
D.17
derives the following approximation error for network implementation:

\displaystyle\quad\left|\mathfrak{N}_{\times}\left({\mathfrak{N}}^{k}_{\rm exp}(x,\widehat{h}_{t},\widehat{\alpha}_{t}),{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm poly}^{k}(x,\widehat{h}_{t},\widehat{\alpha}_{t})\right)-\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\text{Poly}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{\rm prod}+4\max\{\epsilon_{\rm poly},\epsilon_{\rm exp}\},

where
\epsilon_{\rm prod}\in(0,1)
is the target approximation error for the multiplication network
\mathfrak{N}_{\times}(\cdot,\cdot)
. Taking
\epsilon_{\rm prod}=\epsilon_{0}/(4C_{\mathcal{M}})
and
\epsilon_{\rm poly}=\epsilon_{\rm exp}=\epsilon_{0}/(32C_{\mathcal{M}})
gives

\displaystyle\left|\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)-\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\text{Poly}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right|\leq\epsilon_{0}.

(B.38)

Now we combine (
B.36
), (
B.37
) and (
B.38
) to derive

\displaystyle\left|\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)-s_{3}(x,t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)-\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\text{Poly}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\left|\text{Poly}^{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-I_{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)I_{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-s_{3}(x,t)\right|

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+B)^{\gamma_{0}}}{\tau^{\gamma_{0}}}h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon^{\gamma_{0}/\beta}+\epsilon_{0}\right).

According to the network implementation details in Section
B.2.2
and Lemmas F.1-3 in
52
,
\bar{s}_{3}
is
a network in
\mathcal{F}(L_{3},W_{3},S_{3},B_{3},\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L_{3}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(1),\quad W_{3}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(C_{\mathcal{M}}D^{\gamma_{0}}),\quad S_{3}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(C_{\mathcal{M}}D^{\gamma_{0}}),\quad B_{3}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-2(\log(1/\epsilon_{0})+\gamma_{0})/\beta}),

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
hides the logarithmic factors and dependence depends on
d
,
B
,
\beta
and
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}
. Since we take the partition accuracy
r=\epsilon^{1/\beta}
, we have
C_{\mathcal{M}}=\mathcal{O}(r^{-d})=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-d/\beta})
.
∎

B.2.3
Constructing Network Approximation to
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)

Recall the score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
is formulated as

\displaystyle\nabla\log p_{t}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}w_{k}(x,t)\bigg(\frac{\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\mu_{k}}[X_{0}|X_{t}=x]-\Pi_{k}(x,t)}{h_{t}}-\frac{x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)}{h_{t}}\bigg).

Lemma
B.4
establishes the approximation results for the denominator of
w_{k}(x,t)
. Furthermore, we take a more direct approach to approximate the numerator, merge the on-support and orthogonal components together. Specifically, we rewrite
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
as

\displaystyle\nabla\log p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}w_{k}(x,t)\bigg(-\frac{x-\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim\mu_{k}}[X_{0}|X_{t}=x]}{h_{t}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}-\frac{x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}}{h_{t}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})}{\sum_{j=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{j}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{j}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})}

Now we denote

\displaystyle s_{4}(x,t):=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}-\frac{x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0}).

Therefore, we have
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)=s_{4}(x,t)/(\sqrt{h_{t}}s_{3}(x,t))
. Note that
s_{4}(x,t)
differs from
s_{3}(x,t)
with an extra term
(x-\alpha_{t}x_{0})/\sqrt{h_{t}}
inside the integral. Then following the analysis in Lemma
B.4
, we can construct a network
\bar{s}_{4}
to approximate
s_{4}(x,t)
with slightly larger network size. Consequently, there exists a network
\bar{s}_{4}\in\mathcal{F}(L_{4},W_{4},S_{4},B_{4},\cdot)
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{4}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-s_{4}(x,t)\right\|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+B)^{\gamma_{0}}}{\tau^{\gamma_{0}}}h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon^{\gamma_{0}/\beta}+\epsilon_{0}\right),

where we take

\displaystyle L_{4}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(1),\quad W_{4}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(D^{\gamma_{0}+1}\epsilon^{-d/\beta}),\quad S_{4}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(D^{\gamma_{0}+1}\epsilon^{-d/\beta}),\quad B_{4}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-2(\log(1/\epsilon_{0})+\gamma_{0})/\beta}).

Finally, we are ready to prove the score approximation theory for the large noise regime (Lemma
4.5
).
First, we establish the approximation error of
\bar{s}_{4}/(\sqrt{h_{t}}\bar{s}_{3})
to the score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
:

\displaystyle\sqrt{h_{t}}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)-\frac{\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{s_{4}(x,t)}{s_{3}(x,t)}-\frac{\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)}{\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{s_{4}(x,t)}{s_{3}(x,t)}-\frac{s_{4}(x,t)}{\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)}\right\|+\left\|\frac{s_{4}(x,t)-\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)}{\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\sqrt{h_{t}}\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|\cdot\left|\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)-s_{3}(x,t)\right|}{\|\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)\|}+\frac{\left\|s_{4}(x,t)-\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)\right\|}{\|\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)\|}.

For any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, we can derive the bound for
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|\leq\sup_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\left\|\frac{x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}}{h_{t}}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\left(2\sqrt{D\log(1/\epsilon)}+\alpha_{t}B/\sqrt{h_{t}}\right),

as well as the lower bound on
s_{3}(x,t)
,

\displaystyle s_{3}(x,t)\geq\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}:\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|\leq\sqrt{h_{t}}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0})\gtrsim C_{f}^{-1}e^{-1/2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{d}.

Combining all the pieces together, we obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{h_{t}}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)-\frac{\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)}\right\|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}\epsilon^{1/\beta}}{\tau}\right)^{\gamma_{0}}\frac{h_{t}^{d/2}}{h_{t}^{(d+1)/2}}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{h_{t}^{d/2}}\right).

Taking
\epsilon_{0}=h_{t}^{(d-1)/2}\epsilon^{(\beta+1)/\beta}
yields

\displaystyle\sqrt{h_{t}}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)-\frac{\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)}\right\|=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\left(\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}\epsilon^{1/\beta}}{\tau}\right)^{\gamma_{0}}+\epsilon^{(\beta+1)/\beta}\right)\right).

Furthermore, by Lemma F.7 in
52
, there exists a feedforward network
\bar{\phi}
with no more than
\mathcal{O}(\log^{2}(1/\epsilon))
layers, width bounded by
\mathcal{O}(\log^{3}(1/\epsilon))
, at most non-zero
\mathcal{O}(\log^{4}(1/\epsilon))
neurons and weight parameters bounded by
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-2(\beta+1)/\beta})
, such that

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{\phi}\left(\bar{s}_{3}(x,t),\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)\right)-\frac{\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)}\right\|\leq\epsilon_{0}^{(\beta+1)/\beta}.

Now let
\bar{s}_{\rm large}=\bar{\phi}\left(\bar{s}_{3}(x,t),\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)\right)
, so that for any
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bar{\phi}\left(\bar{s}_{3}(x,t),\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)\right)-\frac{\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)}\right\|+\left\|\frac{\bar{s}_{4}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{3}(x,t)}-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\left(\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}\epsilon^{1/\beta}}{\tau}\right)^{\gamma_{0}}+\epsilon^{(\beta+1)/\beta}\right)\right).

Combined with the network size of
\bar{s}_{3}
and
\bar{s}_{4}
, we can conclude

\bar{s}_{\rm large}\in\mathcal{F}(L_{\rm large},W_{\rm large},S_{\rm large},B_{\rm large},\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L_{\rm large}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(1\right),~W_{\rm large}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(D^{\gamma_{0}+1}\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\right),~S_{\rm large}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(D^{\gamma_{0}+1}\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\right),~B_{\rm large}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\epsilon^{-2(\gamma_{0}+\log(1/\epsilon))/\beta}\right).

Finally, we derive the
L^{2}
approximation error of
\bar{s}_{\rm large}
, which can be written as

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(p_{t})}

\displaystyle=\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}}\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}+\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\right)\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x.

Here we can bound the integral within
\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
via the approximation error of
\bar{s}_{\rm large}
.

\displaystyle\int_{x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{h_{t}}\left(\left(\frac{{\log(1/\epsilon)}\epsilon^{2/\beta}}{\tau^{2}}\right)^{\gamma_{0}}+\epsilon^{2(\beta+1)/\beta}\right)\right).

For the integral over
\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left(\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}\right)p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x.

Similar to the small noise case (Appendix
B.1.5
), we clip
\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)
by
R=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)/h_{t}})
via a feedforward layer as constructed in Lemma F.5 of
52
. Them Lemma
E.2
yields

\displaystyle\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x\leq\epsilon^{2(\beta+1)/\beta}\log(1/\epsilon)/h_{t}.

Moreover, (
B.27
) shows

\displaystyle\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon)}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}p_{t}(x){\,\mathrm{d}}x\lesssim\frac{\epsilon^{2(\beta+1)/\beta}}{h_{t}}.

Therefore, we can conclude that the network
\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)
has the following
L^{2}
approximation error for any
t\in[t_{\rm large},T]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(p_{t})}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{h_{t}}\left(\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2/\beta}}{\tau^{2}}\right)^{\gamma_{0}}+\epsilon^{2(\beta+1)/\beta}\right)\right).

B.3
Proof of Theorem
4.4

We prove Theorem
4.4
by constructing a single network that well approximates the score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
for any time
t\in[t_{0},T]
. First, we impose the following conditions on the approximation error
\epsilon>0
so that the small noise and large noise regimes overlap:

\displaystyle\epsilon\leq\min\left\{\frac{\sqrt{\eta\tau}}{rD^{1/4}\sqrt{B}},\frac{\eta\min\{\tau,\sqrt{\tau}\}}{16\max\{\sqrt{D},4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\}},1\right\}^{\beta}.

(B.39)

This requirement on
\epsilon
implies that the small noise regime conditions in (
B.1
) are dominated by
h_{t}\leq\epsilon^{2/\beta}
. Therefore, given
t_{\rm small}=\log\frac{1}{1-\epsilon^{2/\beta}}
and
t_{\rm large}=\log\frac{1}{1-\epsilon^{2/\beta}/4}
, the small noise regime is exactly
[t_{0},t_{\rm small}]
, which overlaps with the large noise regime
[t_{\rm large},T]
in
[t_{\rm large},t_{\rm small}]
.

Next, we construct the time switching network to incorporate networks
\bar{s}_{\rm small}
in Lemma
4.6
and
\bar{s}_{\rm large}
in Lemma
4.5
.
For any
t\in[t_{0},T]
, we define two switching function as

\displaystyle{\rm SW}_{\rm small}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t_{\rm small}-t_{\rm large}}{\rm ReLU}\big((t_{\rm small}-t_{\rm large})-{\rm ReLU}(t-t_{\rm large})+{\rm ReLU}(t-t_{\rm small})\big),

\displaystyle{\rm SW}_{\rm large}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t_{\rm small}-t_{\rm large}}{\rm ReLU}\big({\rm ReLU}(t-t_{\rm large})-{\rm ReLU}(t-t_{\rm small})\big).

Here
{\rm SW}_{\rm small}(t),{\rm SW}_{\rm large}(t)\in[0,1]
,
{\rm SW}_{\rm small}(t)=0
for all
t\geq t_{\rm small}
,
{\rm SW}_{\rm large}(t)=0
for all
t\leq t_{\rm large}
, and
{\rm SW}_{\rm small}(t)+{\rm SW}_{\rm large}(t)=1
for all
t
. Moreover, we note that

\displaystyle{\rm SW}_{\rm small}(t),{\rm SW}_{\rm large}(t)\in\mathcal{F}\left(3,2,8,\max\{t_{\rm small},(t_{\rm small}-t_{\rm large})^{-1}\},\cdot\right).

Now we use the switching functions to construct the score network as

\displaystyle\widetilde{s}(x,t)={\rm SW}_{\rm small}(t)\cdot\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)+{\rm SW}_{\rm large}(t)\cdot\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t).

Here we set
\gamma_{0}=\gamma
in Lemma
4.5
. We will instantiate the choice of
\gamma>0
later.
Utilizing Lemma
D.17
, we can easily implement multiplication and aggregation by feedforward networks. Thereby, the construction
\widetilde{s}(x,t)
yields a network
\bar{s}\in\mathcal{F}(L,W,S,B,\cdot)
with

\displaystyle L=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\gamma^{3}\right),\quad W=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(D^{\gamma}\gamma^{3}\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\right),\quad S=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\gamma D^{\gamma}\epsilon^{-d/\beta}\right),\quad B=\mathcal{O}\left(h_{t_{0}}^{-\gamma}\epsilon^{-2\log(1/\epsilon)/\beta}\right).

Next, we derive the
L^{2}
error of
\bar{s}(x,t)
approximating the score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
when
t\in[t_{0},T]
.

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(p_{t})}

\displaystyle\leq\ind\left(t_{0}\leq t\leq t_{\rm small}\right)\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(p_{t})}

\displaystyle\quad+\ind\left(t_{\rm large}<t\leq T\right)\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(p_{t})}.

Applying the approximation errors in Lemmas
4.6
and
4.5
,

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm small}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{t})}^{2}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{D^{2\gamma+d+2}}{h_{t}}\left(\left(\frac{D\epsilon^{2/\beta}}{\tau^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\right),\quad\text{for any }t\in[t_{0},t_{\rm small}],

and

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}_{\rm large}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{t})}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{h_{t}}\left(\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2/\beta}}{\tau^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}+\epsilon^{2(\beta+1)/\beta}\right)\right),\quad\text{for any }t\in[t_{\rm large},T],

we can derive

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(p_{t})}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{D^{2\gamma+d+2}}{h_{t}}\left(\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2/\beta}}{\tau^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}+\epsilon^{2}\right)\right),\quad\text{for any }t\in[t_{0},T].

In particular, when
\epsilon<\tau^{\beta}
, if we take
\gamma=\lceil\beta(1+\log(\tau^{\beta})/\log(1/\epsilon))^{-1}\rceil
so that
(\epsilon^{1/\beta}/\tau)^{\gamma}\leq\epsilon
, we will have

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{s}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(p_{t})}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{1}{h_{t}}D^{2\gamma+d+2}\epsilon^{2}\right).

Appendix C
Proofs in Section
5

C.1
Proof of Theorem
5.1
