In this proof, we view the training samples as random quantities and slightly abuse the notation to denote
\mathcal{D}=\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, the estimated score function is the empirical risk minimizer defined by

\displaystyle\widehat{s}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{s\in\mathcal{F}}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(s),\quad\text{where }\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(s):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(X_{i};s).

The population risk is denoted as
\mathcal{L}(s)=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P_{\rm data}}[\ell(X;s)]
. According to Theorem
4.4
,
\widehat{s}
can be taken so that
\|\widehat{s}(\cdot,t)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1/\sqrt{h_{t}}
. Thereby we limit
\mathcal{F}
into
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}(L,W,S,B,C_{R}):=\left\{s\in\mathcal{F}(L,W,S,B,R):\|s(\cdot,t)\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{C_{R}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\right\}.

(C.1)

Recall from Section
2
that
\mathcal{L}(s)
is equivalent (up to a constant) to

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(s)=\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P_{\rm data}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|X}\left[\left\|s(X_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]\right]{\,\mathrm{d}}t.

Similarly, we denote the empirical version of
\mathcal{R}(s)
as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(s)=\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|X_{i}}\left[\left\|s(X_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]{\,\mathrm{d}}t.

It is convenient to recenter the empirical loss
\ell
by defining
\ell_{0}
as

\displaystyle\ell_{0}(x;s)=\ell(x;s)-\ell(x;s^{*})\quad\text{with}\quad s^{*}(x,t)=\nabla\log p_{t}(x).

Then we define the centered empirical risk as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(s)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{0}(X_{i};s).

Notably, minimizing
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(s)
is equivalent to minimizing
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(s)
. Moreover, the population centered risk
\mathcal{L}_{0}(s)=\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P_{\rm data}}[\ell_{0}(X;s)]
is simply
\mathcal{R}(s)
, i.e.,
\mathcal{L}_{0}(s)=\mathcal{R}(s)
.

We denote
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}
as the expectation over the randomness in the training samples
\mathcal{D}
. To bound the expected generalization error
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]
, we consider the following decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\bar{s})]}_{\mathrm{(I)}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\bar{s})]}_{\mathrm{(II)}}.

Here, network
\bar{s}
is given in Theorem
4.4
as a constructed approximator to the ground truth score function. For any
\epsilon>0
satisfting (
B.39
), the approximation guarantee in Theorem
4.4
gives rise to

\displaystyle({\rm I})\lesssim D^{2\gamma+d+2}\epsilon^{2},\quad\quad\text{with}\quad\gamma=\left\lceil\frac{\beta\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}}{\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}+\beta\log\tau}\right\rceil.

We will choose
\epsilon
later to optimally balance the error terms. The second term
\mathrm{(II)}
, measuring the difference in generalization errors of
\widehat{s}
and
\bar{s}
, can be further decomposed as

\displaystyle(\textrm{II})

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}\left[\mathcal{L}_{0}(\widehat{s})-\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bar{s})\right]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}\left[\mathcal{L}_{0}(\widehat{s})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(\widehat{s})\right]}_{(\textrm{II-A})}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}\left[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(\widehat{s})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(\bar{s})\right]}_{(\textrm{II-B})}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}\left[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(\bar{s})-\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bar{s})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}\left[\mathcal{L}_{0}(\widehat{s})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(\widehat{s})\right]}_{(\textrm{II-A})}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}\left[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(\widehat{s})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(\bar{s})\right]}_{(\textrm{II-B})},

where equality
(i)
invokes the identity
\mathcal{R}(s)=\mathcal{L}_{0}(s)
, and equality
(ii)
follows since
\bar{s}
is independent of
\mathcal{D}
. By the definition of
\widehat{s}
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(\widehat{s})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(\bar{s})=\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(\widehat{s})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(\bar{s})\leq 0.

Therefore, we have
(\textrm{II-B})\leq 0
and it remains to bound
(\textrm{II-A})
. We introduce a set of i.i.d. ghost samples
\bar{\mathcal{D}}=\{\bar{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
following the same distribution but independent of
\mathcal{D}
. For an arbitrary
a\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle(\textrm{II-A})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}\left[\mathcal{L}_{0}(\widehat{s})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{0}(\widehat{s})-a\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})\right]+a\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P_{\rm data}}[\ell_{0}(X;\widehat{s})]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{0}(X_{i};\widehat{s})-a\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})\right]+a\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D},\bar{\mathcal{D}}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\ell_{0}(\bar{X}_{i};\widehat{s})-\ell_{0}(X_{i};\widehat{s})]-a\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})\right]+a\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D},\bar{\mathcal{D}}}\left[\sup_{s\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\ell_{0}(\bar{X}_{i};s)-\ell_{0}(X_{i};s)\right]-a\mathcal{R}(s)\right]}_{(\spadesuit)}+a\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})],

where equality
(i)
holds since
\bar{\mathcal{D}}
is independent of
\mathcal{D}
, and inequality
(ii)
holds as
\widehat{s}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
. We temporally neglect the
a\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]
term and bound
(\spadesuit)
. Denote
\mathcal{G}=\{\ell_{0}(\cdot;s):s\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}\}
be a function class induced by the score network
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
. For any
\delta>0
, we construct a covering on
\mathcal{G}
with respect to the
L^{\infty}
norm. That is, we select a collection of representative score networks
s_{j}
for
j=1,\dots,\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
, such that for any
s\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
, there exists an
s_{j}
with
\|\ell_{0}(\cdot;s)-\ell_{0}(\cdot;s_{j})\|_{\infty}=\|\ell(\cdot;s)-\ell(\cdot;s_{j})\|_{\infty}\leq\delta
. The count
\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
is known as the covering number of
\mathcal{G}
. Using the covering of
\mathcal{G}
, we can replace the supremum in
(\spadesuit)
by a maximum over a finite set.

For any
s\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
and its close representation
s_{j}
in the covering, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}(s)-R(s_{j})|=|\mathcal{L}_{0}(s)-\mathcal{L}_{0}(s_{j})|=\left|\mathbb{E}_{P_{\rm data}}[\ell_{0}(X;s)-\ell_{0}(X;s_{j})]\right|\leq\delta.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle(\spadesuit)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D},\bar{\mathcal{D}}}\left[\max_{j=1,\dots,\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\ell_{0}(\bar{X}_{i};s_{j})-\ell_{0}(X_{i};s_{j})\right]-a\mathcal{R}(s_{j})\right]+(a+2)\delta.

To further simplify the notation, we denote a variable
h_{i}(s)=\ell_{0}(\bar{X}_{i};s)-\ell_{0}(X_{i};s)
for an arbitrary fixed
s\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
. Note that
\mathbb{E}[h_{i}]=0
and

\displaystyle\Var[h_{i}(s)]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\ell_{0}(\bar{X}_{i};s)-\ell_{0}(X_{i};s)\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\ell(\bar{X}_{i};s)-\ell(\bar{X}_{i};s^{*})-[\ell(X_{i};s)-\ell(X_{i};s^{*})]\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\ell(\bar{X}_{i};s)-\ell(\bar{X}_{i};s^{*})\right|^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\ell(X_{i};s)-\ell(X_{i};s^{*})\right|^{2}\right].

Applying Lemma
C.1
with
C_{\ell}=16(C_{f}C_{R}^{2}+D)
, we have

\displaystyle\Var[h_{i}(s)]\leq C_{\ell}\mathcal{R}(s).

(C.2)

and

\displaystyle|h_{i}(s)|\leq|\ell(\bar{X}_{i};s)|+|\ell(\bar{X}_{i};s^{*})|+|\ell(X_{i};s)|+|\ell(X_{i};s^{*})|\leq C_{\ell}.

Using (
C.2
), we deduce

\displaystyle(\spadesuit)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D},\bar{\mathcal{D}}}\left[\max_{j=1,\dots,\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(h_{i}(s_{j})-\frac{a}{C_{\ell}}\Var[h_{i}(s_{j})]\big)\right]+(a+2)\delta.

(C.3)

For a fixed
s_{j}
and any
\lambda\in(0,3n/C_{\ell})
, by Taylor expansion, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\lambda}{n}h_{i}(s_{j})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[1+\frac{\lambda}{n}h_{i}(s_{j})+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{(\lambda/n)^{k}h_{i}(s_{j})^{k}}{k!}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[1+\frac{\lambda}{n}h_{i}(s_{j})+\frac{h_{i}^{2}(s_{j})(\lambda/n)^{2}}{2}\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{(\lambda/n)^{k-2}C_{\ell}^{k-2}}{3^{k-2}}\right].

where the last inequality follows from
|h_{i}(s)|\leq C_{\ell}
. Summing up the geometric series, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\lambda}{n}h_{i}(s_{j})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[1+\frac{\lambda}{n}h_{i}(s_{j})+\frac{3\lambda^{2}h_{i}^{2}(s_{j})}{6n^{2}-2\lambda nC_{\ell}}\right]

\displaystyle=1+\frac{3\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}[h_{i}^{2}(s_{j})]}{6n^{2}-2\lambda nC_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{3\lambda^{2}\Var[h_{i}(s_{j})]}{6n^{2}-2\lambda nC_{\ell}}\right),

(C.4)

where the equality follows from
\mathbb{E}[h_{i}(s_{j})]=0
and the last inequality invokes the fact
1+x\leq\exp(x)
. Now consider the first term in the right-hand side of (
C.3
). We have

\displaystyle\quad\exp\left(\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D},\bar{\mathcal{D}}}\left[\max_{j=1,\dots,\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(h_{i}(s_{j})-\frac{a}{C_{\ell}}\Var[h_{i}(s_{j})]\big)\right]\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D},\bar{\mathcal{D}}}\left[\exp\left(\max_{j=1,\dots,\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}\frac{\lambda}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(h_{i}(s_{j})-\frac{a}{C_{\ell}}\Var[h_{i}(s_{j})]\big)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1,\ldots,\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D},\bar{\mathcal{D}}}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\lambda}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(h_{i}(s_{j})-\frac{a}{C_{\ell}}\Var[h_{i}(s_{j})]\big)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\sum_{j=1,\dots,\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{3\lambda^{2}}{6n^{2}-2\lambda nC_{\ell}}-\frac{a\lambda}{nC_{\ell}}\right)\Var[h_{i}(s_{j})]\right),

where
(i)
utilizes Jensen’s inequality and
(ii)
invokes (
C.1
). We choose
\lambda=\frac{6an}{(2a+3)C_{\ell}}<\frac{3n}{C_{\ell}}
so that
\frac{3\lambda^{2}}{6n^{2}-2\lambda nC_{\ell}}=\frac{a\lambda}{nC_{\ell}}
, which leads to

\displaystyle\exp\left(\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D},\bar{\mathcal{D}}}\left[\max_{j=1,\dots,\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(h_{i}(s_{j})-\frac{a}{C_{\ell}}\Var[h_{i}(s_{j})]\big)\right]\right)\leq\mathcal{N}(\delta,\mathcal{G},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})

This further implies

\displaystyle(\spadesuit)\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\log\mathcal{N}(\delta)+(a+2)\delta.

Bounding the covering number as
[
52
, Lemma C.2]
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\spadesuit)\lesssim\frac{(2a+3)C_{\ell}}{6an}SL\log(\delta^{-1}LW(B\vee 1)n)+(a+2)\delta.

Substituting
(\spadesuit)
into
(\textrm{II-A})
, we have

\displaystyle(\textrm{II-A})-a\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]\lesssim\frac{(2a+3)C_{\ell}}{6an}SL\log(\delta^{-1}LW(B\vee 1)n)+(a+2)\delta.

Combining the bounds of
(\textrm{II-A})
and
(\textrm{II-B})
yields

\displaystyle(\textrm{II})-a\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]\lesssim\frac{(2a+3)C_{\ell}}{3an}SL\log(\delta^{-1}LW(B\vee 1)n)+(a+2)\delta.

Further combining
(\textrm{I})
and
(\textrm{II})
gives rise to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]-a\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]

\displaystyle=(\textrm{I})+(\textrm{II})-a\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{2\gamma+d+2}\epsilon^{2}+\frac{(2a+3)C_{\ell}}{6an}SL\log(\delta^{-1}LW(B\vee 1)n)+(a+2)\delta.

We set
a=1/2
and derive

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]\lesssim D^{2\gamma+d+2}\epsilon^{2}+\frac{C_{\ell}}{n}SL\log(\delta^{-1}LW(B\vee 1)n)+\delta.

(C.5)

Plugging the network configuration from Theorem
4.4
into (
C.5
) gives rise to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{2\gamma+d+2}\epsilon^{2}+\frac{\gamma^{4}D^{\gamma+1}\epsilon^{-d/\beta}}{n}\left(\log\left(\gamma^{3}D^{\gamma}/\delta\right)+\log(1/(t_{0}\epsilon))\right)+\delta.

We take
\delta=1/n
,
\epsilon=n^{-\beta/(d+2\beta)}
and
t_{0}=n^{-c}
for some constant
c>0
. We simplify the bound on
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}}[\mathcal{R}(\widehat{s})]

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{2\gamma+d+2}n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}+\gamma^{4}D^{\gamma+1}n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\lesssim D^{2\gamma+d+2}n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}.

Moreover, we can rewrite
\gamma
as

\displaystyle\gamma=\left\lceil\frac{\beta\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}}{\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}+\beta\log\tau}\right\rceil=\left\lceil\frac{\beta}{1+(d+2\beta)\log\tau/\log n}\right\rceil.

The proof is complete.

Lemma C.1
.

Suppose Assumption
4.3
holds. Take
t_{0}=e^{-T}
. Then for any
s\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
, with
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
defined in (
C.1
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{M}}\ell(x;s)\leq 4(C_{f}C_{R}^{2}+D),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim P_{\rm data}}|\ell(X_{0};s)-\ell(X_{0};s^{*})|^{2}=16(C_{f}C_{R}^{2}+D)\cdot\mathcal{R}(s),

where
s^{*}(x,t)=\nabla\log p_{t}(x)
is the ground-truth score function.

Proof.

We first derive the uniform bound on
\ell(\cdot;s)
. Since
s\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
,
we have
\|s(\cdot,t)\|_{\infty}\leq C_{R}/\sqrt{h_{t}}
. Then Assumption
4.3
guarantees

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|x_{0}}\|s(X_{t},t)\|^{2}\leq C_{f}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}}\|s(X_{t},t)\|^{2}\leq C_{f}C_{R}^{2}/h_{t}.

(C.6)

By the definition of the loss
\ell
, we have

\displaystyle\ell(x_{0};s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|X_{0}=x_{0}}\left\|s(X_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t}|X_{0})\right\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim N(\alpha_{t}x_{0},h_{t}I_{D})}\left(2\left\|s(X_{t},t)\right\|^{2}+2\left\|\frac{X_{t}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}}{h_{t}}\right\|^{2}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}t.

Using the
L^{2}
-bound on
s
gives

\displaystyle\ell(x_{0};s)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left(\frac{2C_{f}C_{R}^{2}}{h_{t}}+2\mathbb{E}_{z\sim N(0,I_{D})}\frac{\|z\|^{2}}{h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}t=\frac{2(C_{f}C_{R}^{2}+D)}{T-t_{0}}\log(\frac{e^{T}-1}{e^{t_{0}}-1}).

Letting
t_{0}=e^{-T}
, we further have
\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\log\left(\frac{e^{T}-1}{e^{t_{0}}-1}\right)\leq 2
. This yields

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{M}}\ell(x;s)\leq 4(C_{f}C_{R}^{2}+D).

Next, we bound the difference between
s
and
s^{*}
given the same input
x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}
.

\displaystyle\ell(x_{0};s)-\ell(x_{0};s^{*})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|x_{0}}[\|s(X_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x|x_{0})\|^{2}-\|s^{*}(X_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x|x_{0})\|^{2}]{\,\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|x_{0}}\left[(s(X_{t},t)-s^{*}(X_{t},t))^{\top}(s(X_{t},t)+s^{*}(X_{t},t)-2\nabla\log p_{t}(x|x_{0}))\right]{\,\mathrm{d}}t.

By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle|\ell(x_{0};s)-\ell(x_{0};s^{*})|\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|x_{0}}\|s(X_{t},t)-s^{*}(X_{t},t)\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}t}

\displaystyle\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|x_{0}}\|s(X_{t},t)+s^{*}(X_{t},t)-2\nabla\log p_{t}(x|x_{0})\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}t}.

Utilizing the
L^{2}
-bounds of
s
in (
C.6
), we can get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|x_{0}}\|s(X_{t},t)+s^{*}(X_{t},t)-2\nabla\log p_{t}(x|x_{0})\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left(\frac{8C_{f}C_{R}^{2}}{h_{t}}+2\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|x_{0}}\left\|\frac{2(X_{t}-\alpha_{t}x_{0})}{h_{t}}\right\|^{2}\right).

Recall
X_{t}|x_{0}\sim N(\alpha_{t}x_{0},h_{t}I_{D})
. This gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|x_{0}}\|s(X_{t},t)+s^{*}(X_{t},t)-2\nabla\log p_{t}(x|x_{0})\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left(\frac{8C_{f}C_{R}^{2}}{h_{t}}+2\mathbb{E}_{z\sim N(0,I_{D})}\left\|\frac{2\sqrt{h_{t}}z}{h_{t}}\right\|^{2}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left(\frac{C_{f}C_{R}^{2}}{h_{t}}+\mathbb{E}_{z\sim N(0,I_{D})}\frac{\|z\|^{2}}{h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\frac{8}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\frac{C_{R}^{2}+D}{h_{t}}{\,\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=\frac{8(C_{f}C_{R}^{2}+D)}{T-t_{0}}\log\left(\frac{e^{T}-1}{e^{t_{0}}-1}\right).

Letting
t_{0}=e^{-T}
, we further have
\frac{1}{T-t_{0}}\log\left(\frac{e^{T}-1}{e^{t_{0}}-1}\right)\leq 2
. This gives rise to

\displaystyle|\ell(x_{0};s)-\ell(x_{0};s^{*})|^{2}\leq 16\frac{C_{f}C_{R}^{2}+D}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|x_{0}}\|s(X_{t},t)-s^{*}(X_{t},t)\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}t.

Finally, taking expectation over
X_{0}\sim P_{\rm data}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim P_{\rm data}}|\ell(X_{0};s)-\ell(X_{0};s^{*})|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 16\frac{C_{f}C_{R}^{2}+D}{T-t_{0}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim P_{\rm data}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}|X_{0}}\|s(X_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}t

\displaystyle=16(C_{f}C_{R}^{2}+D)\mathcal{R}(s).

The proof is complete.
∎

C.2
Proof of Theorem
5.2

Built upon the score estimation results in Theorem
5.1
, we aim to establish the convergence rate of the estimated distribution
\widehat{P}=\mathrm{Law}(\widehat{Y}_{T-t_{0}})
in
W_{1}
-distance.
Firstly, we introduce
\{\bar{Y}_{t}\}_{t=0}^{T-t_{0}}
that replaces
\widehat{Y}_{0}\sim N(0,I_{D})
by
\bar{Y}_{0}\sim p_{T}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\textrm{d}\bar{Y}_{t}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{1}{2}\bar{Y}_{t}+\widehat{s}(\bar{Y}_{t},T-t)\right]\textrm{d}t+\textrm{d}\overline{B}_{t},\quad\bar{Y}_{0}\sim p_{T}.

By the triangle inequality, we have the following decomposition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(\widehat{P},P_{\rm data})]=\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(\widehat{Y}_{T-t_{0}},X_{0})]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(\widehat{Y}_{T-t_{0}},\bar{Y}_{T-t_{0}})]+\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(\bar{Y}_{T-t_{0}},Y_{T-t_{0}})]+\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(Y_{T-t_{0}},X_{0})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(\widehat{Y}_{T-t_{0}},\bar{Y}_{T-t_{0}})]+\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(\bar{Y}_{T-t_{0}},Y_{T-t_{0}})]+\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(X_{t_{0}},X_{0})].

Let
X\sim P_{\rm data}
and
Z\sim N(0,I_{D})
. Then we have

\displaystyle W_{1}(X_{t_{0}},X_{0})\leq\mathbb{E}\|\alpha_{t}X+\sqrt{h_{t}}Z-X\|\leq(1-\alpha_{t})\mathbb{E}\|X\|+\sqrt{h_{t}}\mathbb{E}\|Z\|\leq\sqrt{D}(B_{\mathcal{M}}+1)\sqrt{h_{t}}\lesssim\sqrt{t_{0}},

where we apply Assumption
4.1
and
h_{t}=1-\alpha_{t}^{2}
. Moreover, combining Theorem D.7 in
52
with Lemmas
4.5
-
4.6
and Theorem
5.1
, we have
\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(\widehat{Y}_{T-t_{0}},\bar{Y}_{T-t_{0}})]\lesssim\exp(-T)
, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(\bar{Y}_{T-t_{0}},Y_{T-t_{0}})]\lesssim\sqrt{n^{-\frac{2}{d+2\beta}}D^{2\gamma+d+2}n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}}+n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{d+2\beta}}\lesssim D^{\gamma+d/2+1}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{d+2\beta}}.

Now combining all the pieces together, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{1}(\widehat{P},P_{\rm data})]\lesssim\sqrt{t_{0}}+\exp(-T)+D^{\gamma+d/2+1}n^{-\frac{\beta+1}{d+2\beta}}.

It suffices to take
t_{0}=n^{-\frac{2(\beta+1)}{d+2\beta}}
and
T=\log n
to conclude the proof.

Appendix D
Omitted Lemmas in Appendix
B

We present formal statements and proofs of supporting lemmas in Appendix
B
.

D.1
Lemma Statements and Proofs in Appendix
B.1.1

Lemma D.1
.

For any fixed time
t>0
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, take
\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)=\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}).
Let
\bar{s}(x,t)
be any function such that
\|\bar{s}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta))}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)/h_{t}}\right)
. Then we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}\|\bar{s}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}P_{t}(x)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{D\log(1/\delta)}{h_{t}}\delta^{2}\right).

Proof of
D.1
.

We begin by applying the the inequality
\|a-b\|^{2}\leq 2(\|a\|^{2}+\|b\|^{2})
to bound the integral:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}\|\bar{s}(x,t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}P_{t}(x)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}\left(\left\|\bar{s}(x,t)\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{t}(x).

Lemma
E.2
bounds the probability mass outside the truncation region. This yields
\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
, which yields,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}\left\|\bar{s}(x,t)\right\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}P_{t}(x)\leq\|\bar{s}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta))}\mathbb{P}\left(x\notin\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)\right)\leq\left\|\bar{s}(x,t)\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\delta^{D}.

Utilizing
\|\bar{s}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta))}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)/h_{t}}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}\backslash\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}\left\|\bar{s}(x,t)\right\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}P_{t}(x)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{h_{t}}\delta^{D}\right).

(D.1)

Recall the marginal density function
p_{t}
satisfies

\displaystyle p_{t}(x)=(2\pi h_{t})^{-D/2}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0}).

and

\displaystyle\nabla p_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=(2\pi h_{t})^{-D/2}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}-\frac{x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}}{h_{t}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x_{0}).

Then we can bound
\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}
as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}(x)\right\|^{2}{\,\mathrm{d}}P_{t}(x)\leq\frac{2}{h_{t}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)}\frac{\|x\|^{2}+DB^{2}}{h_{t}}{\,\mathrm{d}}P_{t}(x)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{D\delta^{2}}{h_{t}}\right).

(D.2)

Combing (
D.1
) and (
D.2
) concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Suppose Assumption
4.2
holds. Let the radius
r<\min\{3\tau,\eta L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\tau)\}
. Consider the atlas
\{(U_{k},\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k})\}_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}
of
\mathcal{M}
given in Remark
2.8
, where
U_{k}=\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r))
. Then the total number of charts
C_{\mathcal{M}}
satisfies

\displaystyle C_{\mathcal{M}}\leq\frac{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{d}T_{d}}{r^{d}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\textrm{d}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}.

Here
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}>0
is the upper bound for the Lipschitz constants of
\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}
’s, and
T_{d}
is the thickness of the charts.

Proof of Lemma
D.2
.

First, we show
\mathcal{B}(x_{k},L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{-1}r)\cap\mathcal{M}\subseteq U_{k}
,
k=1,\ldots,C_{\mathcal{M}}
. For any
z\in\mathcal{B}(x_{k},L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{-1}r)\cap\mathcal{M}
, we have

\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}(z)\|=\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}(z)-\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}(x_{k})\|\leq L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\|z-x_{k}\|\leq r.

(D.3)

Notably,
\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}
is well-defined on
\mathcal{B}(x_{k},L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{-1}r)
according to Assumption
4.2
. Since the exponential map
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
is a diffeomorphism on
\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)
, (
D.3
) implies that
z\in U_{k}
and thus we can conclude
\mathcal{B}(x_{k},L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{-1}r)\cap\mathcal{M}\subseteq U_{k}
. Therefore, we can bound
C_{\mathcal{M}}
with the covering number of a
\ell^{2}
-covering in
\mathbb{R}^{D}
with radius
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{-1}r
. Applying the covering number in
11
with radius
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{-1}r
, we have

C_{\mathcal{M}}\leq\frac{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{d}T_{d}}{r^{d}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\textrm{d}\mu_{\mathcal{M}},

where
T_{d}
is the thickness of the charts.
∎

Lemma D.3
.

For any fixed time
t>0
satisfying (
B.1
), let
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
,
\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
, and
\bar{r}(t)=L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}r+2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}+\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\Delta(t)
. For any
\epsilon_{1}>0
, we have

\displaystyle|f_{1}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|\leq 2h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{1}.

Proof.

Firstly, we clip the integral region by a truncation radius
\Delta_{0}(t)>0
. The resulting truncated integral is

\displaystyle s_{\text{trunc}}(x,t)=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\{v:\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\Delta_{0}(t)\}}

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{h_{t}}\right)\cdot F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Lemma
D.4
derives an upper bound for the clipping error. Take

\displaystyle\Delta_{0}(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)},

and then we have
|s_{\text{trunc}}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|\leq h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{1}
.

The function
s_{\text{trunc}}(x,t)
calculates the sum of truncated integrals over all the charts of
\mathcal{M}
. However, it is sufficient to concentrate on those charts that are in proximity to
x
, out of which the partition of unity
\rho_{k}(x^{*}_{t})=0
so that it does not contribute to the integrals in
s_{\text{trunc}}(x,t)
. Specifically, given an input
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
, we select charts whose center
x_{k}
satisfies

\|x_{k}-x\|\leq L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}r+2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}+\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}+\Delta_{0}(t)=\bar{r}(t).

For the rest of the charts satisfying
\|x_{k}-x\|>\bar{r}(t)
,
taking
x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}
satisfying
\|x_{0}-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\Delta_{0}(t)
, we have

\begin{split}\|x_{0}-x_{k}\|&\geq\|x-x_{k}\|-\|x-x_{0}\|\\
&\geq\|x-x_{k}\|-\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|-\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-x^{*}_{t}\|-\|x^{*}_{t}-x_{0}\|\\
&>\bar{r}(t)-2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}-\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}-\Delta_{0}(t)\\
&>L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}r.\end{split}

(D.4)

This indicates that
x_{0}\notin U_{k}=\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(\mathcal{B}_{T_{x_{k}}\mathcal{M}}(0,r))
, and thus
\rho_{k}(x_{0})=0
as well as
F_{k}(x_{0})=0
. Hence the integrals given in
f_{1}(x,t)
are equal to zero on these charts, so that we can restrict the choice of
k
and only compute integrals on the selected charts.

Furthermore, we show that
x^{*}_{t}\in\mathcal{M}
has a unique preimage under the exponential map with respect to the selected charts. To be specific, for the
k
-th chart satisfying
\|x_{k}-x\|\leq\bar{r}(t)
,
\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}(x^{*}_{t})=\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}^{-1}(x^{*}_{t})
is well-defined. By Assumption
4.2
, it suffices to verify that

\displaystyle\|x^{*}_{t}-x_{k}\|\leq\|x^{*}_{t}-x\|+\|x-x_{k}\|\leq 2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}+\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}+\bar{r}(t)<\eta\tau.

This holds for
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}r<\eta\tau/4
, and time
t>0
such that
2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}+\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}\leq\eta\tau/4
and
\Delta_{0}(t)\leq\eta\tau/4
. By (
B.1
), these conditions are guaranteed to hold if we specify
\delta=\epsilon
to be the ultimate approximation error and
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon
.
Thereby it is valid to define
v_{k}(x,t)=\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}(x^{*}_{t})
on the selected charts where
\|x_{k}-x\|\leq\bar{r}(t)
.

Next, we further shrink each integral region into an
l_{2}
-balls in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, on which the integral is easier to compute. This would give the formulation of
f_{1}
. Intuitively, as a result of the smoothness of the exponential map
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
, any set
V_{k}=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))\|\leq\Delta_{0}(t)\}
has a smooth boundary and thus there exists two
l_{2}
-balls with small difference in radius such that one of them is included in
V_{k}
and the other one contains
V_{k}
. One can verify that

V_{k}^{l}\subseteq V_{k}\subseteq V_{k}^{u},

(D.5)

where
V_{k}^{l}:=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|\leq\Delta(t)\}
with
\Delta(t)=(L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{-1}\Delta_{0}(t)
, and
V_{k}^{u}=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|\leq L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\Delta_{0}(t)\}
. As shown in Lemma
D.5
, we have
|f_{1}(x,t)-s_{\text{trunc}}(x,t)|\leq h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{1}
. Finally, we concludes the proof by adding together the approximation errors,

\displaystyle|f_{1}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|\leq|f_{1}(x,t)-s_{\text{trunc}}(x,t)|+|s_{\text{trunc}}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|\leq 2h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{1}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma D.4
.

For a given
\epsilon_{1}>0
and
t>0
satisfying (
B.1
), let
\{g_{k}\}_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}
be a series of functions satisfying
\|g_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{g}
for some constant
C_{g}>0
, and
\Delta_{0}(t)=c_{0}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
for some constant
c_{0}\geq 2
. Then for any
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\{v:\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|>\Delta_{0}(t)\}}

\displaystyle g_{k}(v)\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v\leq C_{g}h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{1}.

Proof.

For notational simplicity, we denote

\displaystyle(\clubsuit):=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\{v:\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|>\Delta_{0}(t)\}}

\displaystyle g_{k}(v)\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Since
g_{k}
is bounded by
C_{g}>0
, we have

\displaystyle(\clubsuit)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\{v:\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|>\Delta_{0}(t)\}}

\displaystyle C_{g}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

By the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}+2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|\cdot(2\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|-\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|)}{2h_{t}}\right).

If
\|x^{*}_{t}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|>\eta\tau
, we have
\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|>4\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|
. This is because the projection distance
\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|
can be bounded as follows,

\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|\leq 2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}\leq\alpha_{t}\eta\tau/4<\alpha_{t}\|x^{*}_{t}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|/4,

which holds for time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(64D\log(1/\delta)/\eta^{2}+\tau^{2})
. We further obtain that

\displaystyle 2\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|-\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|,

which yields

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}+2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right).

Now we consider the case when
\|x^{*}_{t}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|\leq\eta\tau
. By Assumption
4.2
,
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)
has a unique preimage under
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{x^{*}_{t}}
.
By Lemma
D.7
, the cross-term can be upper bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{h_{t}}\right)\leq\exp\left(\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right).

This implies that

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}+2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right).

Therefore, combining the two cases, we can derive the following bound for
(\clubsuit)
,

\displaystyle(\clubsuit)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|>\Delta_{0}(t)}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}\Delta_{0}(t)^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Notice that

\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int\rho_{k}(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v))p_{\rm data}(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v))G_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}}p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}x_{0}=1,

(D.6)

we can conclude the proof by substituting
\Delta_{0}(t)=c_{0}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
, which gives

(\clubsuit)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}\Delta_{0}(t)^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right)\leq h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{1}.

∎

Lemma D.5
.

Given any
\epsilon_{1}>0
and any time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(256D\log(1/\delta)+\tau^{2})
, let
\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
, and
\{g_{k}\}_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}
be a series of functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
where
|g_{k}|\leq C_{g}
for some constant
C_{g}>0
. Then for any
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\Omega_{k}}

\displaystyle g_{k}(v)\exp\left(-\frac{\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v\leq C_{g}h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{1}.

Here we denote
\Omega_{k}=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\Delta(t)<\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|\leq L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\Delta(t)\}
.

Proof.

First, we apply the upper bound for
g_{k}
and the same arguments in Lemma
D.7
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\Omega_{k}}g_{k}(v)\exp\left(-\frac{\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{g}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\Omega_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Notice that the Lipschitz property of
\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}(\cdot)
indicates

\|v_{k}(x,t)-v\|=\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}(x^{*}_{t})-\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}(x_{0})\|\leq L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\|x^{*}_{t}-x_{0}\|.

(D.7)

Thereby this implies

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))\|=\|x_{0}-x^{*}_{t}\|>(L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}})^{-1}\Delta(t),

for any
v\in\Omega_{k}
. It further gives rise to

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}

\displaystyle\int\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}(L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}})^{-2}\Delta(t)^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Next, we apply (
D.6
) again to bound the above integral:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\Omega_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}(L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}})^{-2}\Delta(t)^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{1},

where the last inequality uses
\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
.
∎

Lemma D.6

(Upper bound for the cross term)
.

For
x\in\alpha_{t}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{M},\tau)
, let
x^{*}_{t}=\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)/\alpha_{t}
. For any
x_{0}\in\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{x^{*}_{t}}(\mathcal{B}_{T_{x^{*}_{t}}\mathcal{M}}(0,{\tt inj}(\mathcal{M})))
and
t>0
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|-\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},x^{*}_{t}-x_{0}\rangle\big\|\leq\frac{2}{\tau}\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|\cdot\|x^{*}_{t}-x_{0}\|^{2}.

Proof.

Consider a curve
\gamma:(-a,a)\to\mathcal{M}
from some constant
a>0
, such that
\gamma(0)=x^{*}_{t}
,
\gamma(\tau)=x_{0}
and
\gamma^{\prime}(0)\in T_{x^{*}_{t}}\mathcal{M}
. Moreover,
\|\gamma^{\prime}(s)\|=1
for any
s\in(-a,a)
.
Then we can derive the cross term as following:

\displaystyle-\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},x^{*}_{t}-x_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\gamma(\tau)-\gamma(0)\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\int_{0}^{\tau}\gamma^{\prime}(s){\,\mathrm{d}}s\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\int_{0}^{\tau}[\gamma^{\prime}(s)-\gamma^{\prime}(0)]{\,\mathrm{d}}s+\tau\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\rangle.

It is demonstrated in Lemma
E.1
that
x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}
is perpendicular to
T_{x^{*}_{t}}\mathcal{M}
. Hence we have
\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\gamma^{\prime}(0)\rangle=0
. It follows that

\displaystyle\big\|-\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},x^{*}_{t}-x_{0}\rangle\big\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\Big\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\int_{0}^{\tau}[\gamma^{\prime}(s)-\gamma^{\prime}(0)]{\,\mathrm{d}}s\Big\rangle\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|\cdot\left|\int_{0}^{\tau}[\gamma^{\prime}(s)-\gamma^{\prime}(0)]{\,\mathrm{d}}s\right|,

where the last inequality uses Cauchy-Schwartz inequality.
Moreover, by Proposition 6.1 in
51
, we have
\|\gamma^{\prime\prime}(s)\|\leq 1/\tau
where
\tau
is the reach of manifold
\mathcal{M}
. Then we arrive at

\displaystyle\big\|-\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},x^{*}_{t}-x_{0}\rangle\big\|\leq\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|\cdot\left|\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{s}{\tau}{\,\mathrm{d}}s\right|\leq\frac{\tau^{2}}{2\tau}\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|.

Furthermore, we apply Proposition 6.3 in
51
to get

\tau\leq\tau-\tau\sqrt{1-\frac{2\|x^{*}_{t}-x_{0}\|}{\tau}}\leq 2\|x^{*}_{t}-x_{0}\|.

Combining the above inequalities concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.7
.

Let
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
and
v\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),{\tt inj}(\mathcal{M}))
. Then for any time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(256D\log(1/\delta)+\tau^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{h_{t}}\leq\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}.

Proof.

Using the upper bound for the cross term given in Lemma
D.6
, we get

\displaystyle-\frac{\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{h_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha_{t}}{\tau h_{t}}\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|\cdot\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle\frac{16\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}}{\alpha_{t}\tau}\cdot\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}.

The last inequality uses
\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|\leq 2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}
. Finally, we conclude the proof by applying
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(256D\log(1/\delta)+\tau^{2})
, which is equivalent to
16\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}\leq\alpha_{t}\tau
.
∎

Lemma D.8
.

For any time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq r^{2}/(256D\log(1/\delta)+\tau^{2})
, let
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
and
\{g_{k}\}_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}
be a series of functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
where
|g_{k}|\leq C_{g}
for some constant
C_{g}>0
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}g_{k}(v)\exp\left(-\frac{2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 122.85876pt\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\cdot\left[\widehat{F}_{k}(v)-F_{k}(v)\right]{\,\mathrm{d}}v\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(4\pi L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}h_{t}/(\alpha_{t}^{2})\right)^{d/2}C_{F}C_{g}d^{\beta}r^{\beta}|\mathcal{I}(x)|.

Proof.

For notational simplicity, denote

\displaystyle(\clubsuit)=\Bigg|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}g_{k}(v)\exp\left(-\frac{2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\cdot\left[\widehat{F}_{k}(v)-F_{k}(v)\right]{\,\mathrm{d}}v\Bigg|.

Due to Lemma
D.7
and the condition that
g_{k}
is upper bounded, we have

\displaystyle(\clubsuit)\leq C_{g}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right)\cdot\left|\widehat{F}_{k}(v)-F_{k}(v)\right|{\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Moreover, we apply the bound for
\left|\widehat{F}_{k}(v)-F_{k}(v)\right|
stated in (
B.11
), which leads to

\displaystyle(\clubsuit)\leq C_{g}C_{F}d^{\beta}r^{\beta}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Since the Logarithm map is
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}
-Lipschitz, we further have

\displaystyle(\clubsuit)

\displaystyle\leq C_{g}C_{F}d^{\beta}r^{\beta}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\exp\left(-\frac{(L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}})^{-2}\alpha_{t}^{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle=C_{g}C_{F}d^{\beta}r^{\beta}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\left(\frac{4\pi L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}h_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\right)^{d/2}\mathbb{P}\left(\|Z\|\leq\frac{\alpha_{t}\Delta(t)}{\sqrt{2h_{t}}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}}\right),

where the last equality holds for the standard Gaussian random variable
Z\sim N(0,I_{d})
. Furthermore, notice that the probability is always no more that
1
and the number of charts is
C_{\mathcal{M}}
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{4h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{I}(x)|\left(\frac{4\pi L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}h_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\right)^{d/2}.

In conclusion, we put all the inequalities together and then arrive at

\displaystyle(\clubsuit)\leq\left(4\pi L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}h_{t}/(\alpha_{t}^{2})\right)^{d/2}C_{F}C_{g}d^{\beta}r^{\beta}|\mathcal{I}(x)|.

∎

Lemma D.9
.

Fix any
\epsilon_{1}\in[0,e^{-1}]
and time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(64D\log(1/\delta)+\tau^{2})
. Let
\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
. For any
\epsilon_{3}>0
and
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
, if we take
\gamma\in(0,\beta]
as some fixed constant and
\gamma^{\prime}\geq 4e^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}d\log(1/h_{t})/2+4e^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+\log(1/\epsilon_{3})
, we have that

\displaystyle|f_{3}(x,t)-f_{2}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{F}\frac{(4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}\pi h_{t}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2))^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)(L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\alpha_{t})^{d}}|\mathcal{I}(x)|

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\gamma!}\left(\frac{16L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}{\alpha_{t}\tau}\right)^{\gamma}+\epsilon_{3}\right).

Proof.

Given that the exponential function is
C^{\infty}
, we can approximate it up to any order. To facilitate the approximation, we firstly bound the cross term inside the exponential function by Lemma
D.6
. Recall by definition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{k}=\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{D}_{k}=\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}.

Here we write
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}={\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,v,t)
and
\mathcal{D}_{k}=\mathcal{D}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)
for notational simplicity.
By Lemma
D.6
, for
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
, we have

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{T}}_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\tau}\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|\cdot\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)}}{2\tau}\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}.

Now we apply
8\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log 1/\delta}\leq\alpha_{t}\tau
in the last inequality, i.e.
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(64D\log(1/\delta)+\tau^{2})
,

\displaystyle\left|{\mathcal{T}}_{k}\right|\leq\frac{\alpha_{t}}{2}\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}=\frac{\alpha_{t}}{2}|\mathcal{D}_{k}|,

Take
\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
. Then for
v\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\alpha_{t}}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}\right|\leq 2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2).

(D.8)

This further indicates the following upper bound for the exponential of the cross term:

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}\right)\leq\exp\left(2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)\right)=h_{t}^{-L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}d}\epsilon_{1}^{-2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}}.

Next, if we approximate the exponential function with the cross term up to an order
\gamma
,
the tail can be bounded by Lemma
E.3
as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bigg|\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}\right)-g_{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{T}}_{k})\bigg|&\leq\frac{1}{\gamma!}\left(\frac{4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}\alpha_{t}\sqrt{D\log(1/\delta)}\Delta(t)^{2}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}\tau}\right)^{\gamma}\exp\left(\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}|\mathcal{D}_{k}|\right),\end{split}

(D.9)

where we denote

g_{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{T}}_{k}):=\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{(-1)^{j}}{j!(h_{t}/\alpha_{t})^{j}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{j}.

(D.10)

Based on (
D.8
), we can derive the following upper bound for
g_{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{T}}_{k})
:

\displaystyle|g_{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{T}}_{k})|\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{1}{j!}\left|\frac{\alpha_{t}}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{C}\right|^{j}\leq\left(2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)\right)^{\gamma}.

(D.11)

Likewise, we can approximate the exponential function with
\mathcal{D}_{k}
up to an order
\gamma^{\prime}\geq\log(1/\epsilon_{3})+4e^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}d\log(1/h_{t})+8e^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}\log(1/\epsilon_{1})
. We again apply Lemma
E.3
by setting
\epsilon=h_{t}^{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}d}\epsilon_{1}^{2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}}\epsilon_{3}
to bound the approximation error:

\displaystyle\bigg|\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{2}\right)-g_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{D}_{k})\bigg|\leq h_{t}^{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}d}\epsilon_{1}^{2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}}\epsilon_{3},

(D.12)

where we denote

\quad g_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{D}_{k}):=\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}l!(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{l}.

Next, we proceed to bound the difference between
f_{3}
and
f_{2}
.

\displaystyle|f_{3}(x,t)-f_{2}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{F}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\bigg|g_{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{T}}_{k})g_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{D}_{k})-\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}\mathcal{D}_{k}\right)\bigg|{\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{F}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\bigg[\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\bigg|\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}\right)-g_{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{T}}_{k})\bigg|\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}\mathcal{D}_{k}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\bigg|\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}\mathcal{D}_{k}\right)-g_{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{D}_{k})\bigg|\cdot g_{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{T}}_{k}){\,\mathrm{d}}v\bigg].

Plug in (
D.9
), (
D.11
) and (
D.12
), then we get

\displaystyle|f_{3}(x,t)-f_{2}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{F}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma!}\left(\frac{4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}\alpha_{t}\sqrt{D\log(1/\delta)}\Delta(t)^{2}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}\tau}\right)^{\gamma}+\epsilon_{3}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{F}\frac{\pi^{d/2}(\Delta(t))^{d}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)}\left(\frac{1}{\gamma!}\left(\frac{4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}\alpha_{t}\sqrt{D\log(1/\delta)}\Delta(t)^{2}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}\tau}\right)^{\gamma}+\epsilon_{3}\right)|\mathcal{I}(x)|.

We conclude the proof by applying
\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
.
∎

Lemma D.10
.

Fix any
\epsilon_{2}>0
, and take
r=\epsilon_{2}^{1/\beta}
.
We have for any
v,v_{k}(x,t)\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)
,

\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)\right|\leq\left(L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}+\sqrt{D}C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}\right)^{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2},

and

\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)-\mathcal{D}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)\right|\leq DC_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}(C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}+2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}.

Here
C_{d,\beta}>0
is a constant depending on
d
and
\beta
.

Proof.

In this proof, we omit the order
S=\beta+1
in
\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k}^{\beta+1}
for notational simplicity. We first apply Lemma
F.2
with
p=1
,

\max_{\|\theta\|\leq 1}\left|\partial^{\theta}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}\right)\right|_{L_{\infty}(\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r))}\leq C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}.

(D.13)

Since
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
is
L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}
-Lipschitz, we can further upper bound the Lipschitz constant of
\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k}
:

\mathrm{\text{Lip}}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k}\right)\leq L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}+\sqrt{D}C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}.

(D.14)

Therefore, by utilizing this Lipschitz continuity of
\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k}
, we can get

\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)\right|=\left\|\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t))-\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k}(v)\right\|^{2}\leq\left(L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}+\sqrt{D}C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}\right)^{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}.

Next, we bound the approximate error for
\widehat{D}_{k}
. To begin with, we rewrite the approximate error as follows:

\displaystyle\quad~\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)-\mathcal{D}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{D}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v)-\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v_{k}(x,t))\right)^{2}-\left(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(v)-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(v_{k}(x,t))\right)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{D}\underbrace{\left|\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v)-\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(v)+\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(v_{k}(x,t))\right|}_{\text{(I)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdot\underbrace{\left|\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v)-\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v_{k}(x,t))+\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(v)-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(v_{k}(x,t))\right|}_{\text{(II)}}.

Notice that (I) can be upper bounded by the Lipschitz continuity of
\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}
:

(I)

\displaystyle=\left|\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v)-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(v)\right)-\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(v_{k}(x,t))\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{\text{Lip}}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}\right)\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|.

On the other hand, term (II) can be bounded by the smoothness of
\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}
and
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}
:

(II)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathrm{\text{Lip}}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}\right)+\mathrm{\text{Lip}}\left(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}\right)\right)\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathrm{\text{Lip}}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}\right)+2\mathrm{\text{Lip}}\left(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}\right)\right)\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|.

Moreover, (
D.13
) implies that

\mathrm{\text{Lip}}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}\right)\leq C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)-\mathcal{D}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq DC_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}(C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}+2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma D.11
.

Fix any
\epsilon_{2}\in(0,\epsilon_{1})
where
\epsilon_{1}
is given in Lemma
D.4
. Take
r=\epsilon_{2}^{1/\beta}
. Given time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq\tau/(256D\log(1/\delta)+\tau)
, let
\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
. For any
\epsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
and
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
,
we have

|f_{4}(x,t)-f_{3}(x,t)|\leq 3\gamma^{\prime}DC_{d,\beta}C_{F}\frac{\left(\pi h_{t}/2\right)^{d/2}}{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{d}\alpha_{t}^{d}}\left(\frac{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}{\sqrt{\tau}}\right)^{\beta}\epsilon_{2}|I(x)|.

Proof.

As shown in (
B.11
),
\widehat{F}_{k}(v)
can be always bounded by
2C_{F}
as long as
r
is sufficiently small. This implies that

\displaystyle\quad~|f_{4}(x,t)-f_{3}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{F}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\left|\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}\cdot l!}\frac{\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}-{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}\right|g_{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{T}}_{k}){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

where
g_{\mathcal{T}}
is defined in (
D.10
). Combined with (
D.11
), the above inequality can be further derived as

\displaystyle|f_{4}(x,t)-f_{2}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{F}\left(\frac{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}{\sqrt{\tau}}\right)^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\left|\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}\cdot l!}\frac{\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}-{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}\right|{\,\mathrm{d}}v.

By Lemma
D.10
, we can choose
\epsilon_{2}\leq L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}/C_{d,\beta}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}-{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}\right|\leq 3DC_{d,\beta}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon_{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}.

Notice that for any
p,q>0
, we have

\displaystyle(p+q)^{l}-p^{l}=\sum_{i=0}^{l}{l\choose i}q^{i}p^{l-i}-p^{l}=\sum_{i=1}^{l}{l\choose i}q^{i}p^{l-i}\leq lq\sum_{i=1}^{l}{l-1\choose i-1}q^{i-1}p^{l-i}=lq(p+q)^{l-1}.

This suggests that we can bound
\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}-{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}\right|
by

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}-{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 3lDC_{d,\beta}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon_{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}\cdot(2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{2(l-1)}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2(l-1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2}lDC_{d,\beta}(2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{2l}\epsilon_{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2l},

where we plug in
p={\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)
and
p+q=\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)
and use
|p+q|=|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)|\leq(2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}
. Thereby we can get

\displaystyle\quad~\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\left|\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}\cdot l!}\frac{\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}-{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}\right|}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}\right|{\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{\frac{3}{2}lD(2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{2l}\epsilon_{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2l}}{2^{l}\cdot l!(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}{\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}\gamma^{\prime}DC_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\exp\left(\frac{(2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{2}\alpha_{t}^{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Notably, the integral can be written as an integration of normal density and thus we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\exp\left(\frac{(2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{2}\alpha_{t}^{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}v\leq\left(\frac{\pi h_{t}}{2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}\alpha_{t}^{2}}\right)^{d/2}.

(D.15)

Finally, we plug in the choice of
\Delta(t)
and obtain

\displaystyle|f_{4}(x,t)-f_{3}(x,t)|\leq 3\gamma^{\prime}DC_{d,\beta}C_{F}\frac{\left(\pi h_{t}/2\right)^{d/2}}{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{d}\alpha_{t}^{d}}\left(\frac{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}{\sqrt{\tau}}\right)^{\gamma}\epsilon_{2}|\mathcal{I}(x)|.

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma D.12
.

Fix any
\epsilon_{2}>0
, and take
r=\epsilon_{2}^{1/\beta}
.
We have for any
v,v_{k}(x,t)\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)
,

\|[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}\|_{\infty}\leq\left(L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}+\sqrt{D}C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}\right)^{j}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{j},

and

\|[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}-[\Delta_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}\|_{\infty}\leq jC_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}(C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}+2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{j-1}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{j},

which hold for any
z,z+v-v_{k}(x,t)\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)
.

Proof.

In the proof, we omit the order
S=\beta+1
in
\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k}^{\beta+1}
for notational simplicity. Let
I\in\{1,\ldots,D\}^{d}
be a multi-index. We utilize the Lipschitz continuity of
\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k}
given in (
D.14
) and get

\left|[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]_{I}^{\otimes j}\right|=\prod_{i\in I}\left|\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v)-\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v)\right|\leq\left(L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}+\sqrt{D}C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}\right)^{j}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{j}.

Next, we bound the approximate error for
[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}
. Notice that the approximate error can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\left|[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}_{I}-[\Delta_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}_{I}\right|

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left|\prod_{i\in I}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v_{k}(x,t))-\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}(v)\right)-\prod_{i\in I}\left(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(v_{k}(x,t))-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}(v)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle j\mathrm{\text{Lip}}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}\right)\left(\mathrm{\text{Lip}}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k,i}\right)+\mathrm{\text{Lip}}\left(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}\right)\right)^{j-1}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{j}.

where the last inequality follows
\prod_{i=1}^{j}a_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{j}b_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{j}a_{1}\cdots a_{i-1}(a_{i}-b_{i})b_{i+1}\cdots b_{j}
.
Following the same analysis in Lemma
D.10
, we can get

\displaystyle\left|[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]_{I}^{\otimes j}-[\Delta_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]_{I}^{\otimes j}\right|\leq jC_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}(C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}+2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{j-1}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{j}.

We conclude the proof by applying the entry-wise results to the
\ell^{\infty}
-bounds.
∎

Lemma D.13
.

Fix any
\epsilon_{2}\in(0,2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}/C_{d,\beta})
and take
r=\epsilon_{2}^{1/\beta}
. Let
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
,
\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
. For any time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq\tau/(256D\log(1/\delta)+\tau)
,
we have

\displaystyle|f_{5}(x,t)-f_{4}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{d,\beta}C_{F}(\pi h_{t}/2)^{d/2}}{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{d+1}(\alpha_{t})^{d}}\gamma D^{\gamma/2}(36L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2))^{\gamma/2}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}\epsilon_{2}.

Proof.

For notational simplicity, define

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{k,j}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t):=[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}-[\Delta_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}.

As shown in Lemma
D.9
,
\widehat{F}_{k}(v)
can be always bounded by
2C_{F}
. By Lemma
D.10
, we can infer the following bound:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}l!}\frac{\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)^{l}}{(h_{t}/\alpha^{2}_{t})^{l}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{1}{2^{l}l!}\frac{\left|\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)\right|^{l}}{(h_{t}/\alpha^{2}_{t})^{l}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}\alpha_{t}^{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{h_{t}}\right),

which holds for
\epsilon_{2}\leq L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}/\sqrt{D}
and
\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|\leq\Delta(t)
. Therefore, the difference between
f_{5}
and
f_{4}
can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle|f_{5}(x,t)-f_{4}(x,t)|\leq 2C_{F}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{(\alpha_{t})^{j}}{j!(h_{t})^{j}}\left\|[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]^{\otimes j}\right\|

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\left\|\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{k,j}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)\right\|\exp\left(\frac{2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}\alpha_{t}^{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}v,

where we apply Cauchy-Schwartz inequality. Here the norm of tensor can be computed as

\left\|[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]^{\otimes j}\right\|=\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}.

Moreover, by Lemma
D.12
, choosing
\epsilon_{2}\leq 2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}/C_{d,\beta}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{k,j}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq D^{\gamma/2}\|[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}-[\Delta_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq jD^{\gamma/2}C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}(3L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{j-1}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{j},

Combine the above inequalities, we can derive that

\displaystyle|f_{5}(x,t)-f_{4}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{F}\gamma D^{\gamma/2}C_{d,\beta}\epsilon_{2}\frac{(3L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})^{\gamma-1}\alpha_{t}^{\gamma}(\Delta(t))^{\gamma}}{h_{t}^{\gamma/2}}\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{h_{t}^{j/2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}\exp\left(\frac{2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}\alpha_{t}^{2}\|v-v_{k}(x,t)\|^{2}}{h_{t}}\right){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Finally, we plug in (
D.15
) and the choice of
\Delta(t)
. Then we obtain that

\displaystyle|f_{5}(x,t)-f_{4}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{d,\beta}C_{F}(\pi h_{t}/2)^{d/2}}{L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{d+1}(\alpha_{t})^{d}}\cdot\gamma D^{\gamma/2}(36L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2))^{\gamma/2}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\max_{j=0,1,\ldots,\gamma}\left\{\frac{\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}}{(h_{t})^{j/2}}\right\}|\mathcal{I}(x)|\epsilon_{2}.

The proof is complete.
∎

D.2
Helper Lemmas in Section
B.1.2

Lemma D.14

(Network Implementation for Low-dimensional Representation)
.

Fix time
t>0
such that
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(4D\log(1/\delta)+\tau^{2})
. Let
\bar{r}(t)\leq 1/2
. Given an input
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
, for
k
-th chart whose center satisfies
\|x_{k}-x\|\leq\bar{r}(t)
,
v_{k}
can be well approximated by a network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}=({\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},1},{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},2},\ldots,{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},d})
, that is, for
\epsilon_{v}>0
, we have

\left\|{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}(x,t)-v_{k}(x,t)\right\|\leq\epsilon_{v}.

Here each
{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},i}
is a feedforward network with depth at most
c\log(1/\epsilon_{v})
, width and number of weights bounded by
c\epsilon_{v}^{-d/\beta}\log(1/\epsilon_{v})
and the range of weights bounded by
c\max\{B_{\mathcal{M}},\tau^{2}\}
, where
c>0
is some constant at most polynomially depending on
D,d,\beta
and the smoothness of
\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k}\circ\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.14
.

Take the approximation degree to be
(D+1)\beta/d
in Theorem 1 in
11
. There exists a ReLU feedforward network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},i}
with depth at most
c\log(1/\epsilon_{v})
, width and number of weights bounded by
c\epsilon_{v}^{-d/\beta}\log(1/\epsilon_{v})
and the range of weights bounded by
c\max\{B_{\mathcal{M}},\tau^{2}\}
, such that

\left|{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},i}(x,t)-v_{k,i}(x,t)\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon_{v}}{d}.

Then we have

\left\|{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}(x,t)-v_{k}(x,t)\right\|=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left|{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},i}(x,t)-v_{k,i}(x,t)\right|^{2}}\leq\epsilon_{v}.

The proof is complete by setting
{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}=({\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},1},{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},2},\ldots,{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k},d})
.
∎

Lemma D.15
.

For time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(64D\log(1/\delta)+\tau^{2})
and
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
, let
\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
. Then for the approximation error
\epsilon_{v}>0
given in Lemma
D.14
and
x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\delta)
, we have

\displaystyle|f_{6}(x,t)-f_{5}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\text{poly}}\left(\frac{h_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\left(4D^{2}\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\bigg(4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}\bigg(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}+\frac{d}{2}\log\frac{1}{h_{t}}\bigg)\bigg)^{(\gamma+d)/2+\gamma^{\prime}-1}|\mathcal{I}(x)|\epsilon_{v}.

Here
C_{poly}
is a the same constant introduced in Lemma
D.16
, which only depends on
d
,
R
,
\beta
,
C_{F}
and the smoothness of exponential maps
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
.

Proof.

According to Lemma
D.16
, given a fixed
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
, the Lipschitz constant of
f_{5}(x,t)
with respect to
v_{k}(x,t)
are bounded by

\displaystyle\mathrm{\text{Lip}}(f_{5})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{D^{j/2}}{j!(h_{t})^{j/2}}\left\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\right\|^{j}\cdot\max_{I}\mathrm{\text{Lip}}(\text{Tensor-Poly}_{I}^{k,j})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}(x)}\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{(4D^{2}\log(1/\delta))^{j/2}}{j!}\cdot\max_{I}\mathrm{\text{Lip}}(\text{Tensor-Poly}_{I}^{k,j})

\displaystyle\leq C_{\text{poly}}\left(\frac{h_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\right)^{d/2}(4D^{2}\log(1/\delta))^{\gamma/2}\left({4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}\right)^{(\gamma+d)/2+\gamma^{\prime}-1}|\mathcal{I}(x)|.

Utilizing the above Lipschitz property, we have

\displaystyle|f_{6}(x,t)-f_{5}(x,t)|\leq\mathrm{\text{Lip}}(f_{5})\|{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}(x,t)-v_{k}(x,t)\|.

Plugging in the approximation error
\|{\mathfrak{N}}_{v_{k}}(x,t)-v_{k}(x,t)\|\leq\epsilon_{v}
given in Lemma
D.14
concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.16
.

Given any time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(64D\log(1/\delta)+\tau^{2})
and
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
, let

\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
. Then for
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
, each entry of

\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}
is a polynomial with respect to
v_{k}(x,t)
with degree at most
(\beta+1)(j+\gamma^{\prime}+1)
. Moreover, there exists a constant
C_{\text{poly}}>0
only depending on
d
,
r
,
\beta
,
C_{F}
and the smoothness of exponential maps
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
, such that
each entry of

\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}
is Lipschitz with respect to
v_{k}(x,t)
with Lipschitz constant and its coefficients with respect to
v_{k}(x,t)
bounded by

\displaystyle C_{\text{poly}}\left(\frac{h_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\right)^{d/2}\left({4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}\right)^{(j+d)/2+\gamma^{\prime}-1}.

Proof.

Recall the formulation of
f_{5}(x,t)
in (
B.16
), where we defined

\displaystyle\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}(v_{k}(x,t),t)

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(v_{k}(x,t),\Delta(t))}

\displaystyle\frac{[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t)]^{\otimes j}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{j/2}}\cdot\left[\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}l!}\frac{(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{k}(v_{k}(x,t),v,t))^{l}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}\right]\cdot\widehat{F}_{k}(v)\textrm{d}v.

Substituting the integration variable
v
by
u=v-v_{k}(x,t)
, we can rewrite
\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}
as

\displaystyle\text{Tensor-Poly}^{k,j}(v_{k}(x,t),t)=\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,\Delta(t))}

\displaystyle\frac{[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),u,t)]^{\otimes j}}{u^{j}}\frac{u^{j}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{j/2}}\cdot\widehat{F}_{k}(u+v_{k}(x,t))

\displaystyle\cdot\bigg[\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}l!}\bigg(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{D}\big(\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}}(v_{k}(x,t),u,t)\big)^{2}}{u^{2}}\bigg)^{l}\frac{u^{2l}}{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})^{l}}\bigg]{\,\mathrm{d}}u,

where
\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),u,t)=\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k}^{\beta+1}(v_{k}(x,t))-\widehat{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}_{k}^{\beta+1}(u+v_{k}(x,t))
.
Denoting
\mathcal{P}^{k,j}(v_{k}(x,t),t)
as follows,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{k,j}(v_{k}(x,t),t)=\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,\Delta(t))}

\displaystyle\frac{[\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}}(v_{k}(x,t),u,t)]^{\otimes j}}{u^{j}}\cdot\widehat{F}_{k}(u+v_{k}(x,t))

\displaystyle\cdot\bigg[\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}}{2^{l}l!}\bigg(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{D}\big(\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k,i}}(v_{k}(x,t),u,t)\big)^{2}}{u^{2}}\bigg)^{l}\bigg]{\,\mathrm{d}}u,

we notice that for a multi-index
I\in\{1,\ldots,D\}^{2}
,
\text{Tensor-Poly}_{I}^{k,j}
and
\mathcal{P}^{k,j}_{I}(v_{k}(x,t))
have the same degree with respect to
v_{k}(x,t)
. The bound for the coefficients of
\text{Tensor-Poly}_{I}^{k,j}
, denoted as
\mathrm{\text{coef}}(\text{Tensor-Poly}_{I}^{k,j})
, can be controlled by

\mathrm{\text{coef}}(\mathcal{P}^{k,j}_{I})\left(\frac{\Delta(t)}{\sqrt{h_{t}}/\alpha_{t}}\right)^{j+2(\gamma^{\prime}-1)}.

Likewise, the Lipschitz constant of
\text{Tensor-Poly}_{I}^{k,j}
, denoted as
\mathrm{\text{Lip}}(\text{Tensor-Poly}_{I}^{k,j})
, can be bounded by

\mathrm{\text{Lip}}(\mathcal{P}_{I}^{k,j})\left(\frac{\Delta(t)}{\sqrt{h_{t}}/\alpha_{t}}\right)^{j+2(\gamma^{\prime}-1)}.

By Lemma
D.10
and
D.12
, and the calculation of polynomial size in Section
G
,
\mathcal{P}_{I}^{k,j}
has degree at most
(\beta+1)(j+\gamma^{\prime}+1)
with respect to
v_{k}(x,t)
, and there exists a constant
C_{\text{poly}}>0
, which only depends on
d
,
r
,
\beta
,
C_{F}
and the smoothness of exponential maps
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
, such that

\mathrm{\text{coef}}(\mathcal{P}^{k,j}_{I})\leq C_{\text{poly}}(\Delta(t))^{d},

and
\mathcal{P}^{k,j}_{I}
is approximately Lipschitz with constant

\displaystyle\mathrm{\text{Lip}}(\mathcal{P}^{k,j}_{I})\leq C_{\text{poly}}(\Delta(t))^{d}.

Therefore,
\text{Tensor-Poly}_{I}^{k,j}
has degree at most
(\beta+1)(j+\gamma^{\prime}+1)
and its coefficients and Lipschitz constant can be respectively bounded by

\displaystyle C_{\text{poly}}\left(\frac{h_{t}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\right)^{d/2}\left({4L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}\right)^{(j+d)/2+\gamma^{\prime}-1},

where we plug in
\Delta(t)=2L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}\sqrt{(h_{t}/\alpha_{t}^{2})(\log(1/\epsilon_{1})+d\log(1/h_{t})/2)}
.
∎

Lemma D.17

(Network Implementation for Tensor Product)
.

Let
C\geq 1
. Given any
j\in\{1,\ldots,\gamma-1\}
, and
\epsilon_{\times}\in(0,1)
, there exists a ReLU feedforward networks
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\times}^{j}
, with
\mathcal{O}(\log(1/\epsilon_{\times})+\log C)
layers, width
\mathcal{O}(D^{j})
,
\mathcal{O}(D^{j}(\log(1/\epsilon_{\times})+\log C))
non-zero neurons, and weight parameters upper bounded by
C^{2}
such that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathfrak{N}}_{\times}^{j}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2})-\left\langle v_{1}^{\otimes j},v_{2}^{\otimes j}\right\rangle\right|\leq D^{j}\epsilon_{\times}+2CD^{j}\epsilon_{\text{error}},

which holds for all
v_{1},v_{2}\in[-C,C]^{D}
, and
j
-th order width-
D
tensors
{\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
satisfying
\|{\mathcal{T}}_{i}-v_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\epsilon_{\text{error}}
,
i=1,2
.

Proof of Lemma
D.17
.

Recall the definition of the product of
j
-th order tensors, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle v_{1}^{\otimes j},v_{2}^{\otimes j}\right\rangle=\left\langle v_{1},v_{2}\right\rangle^{j}=\sum_{I_{1},\ldots,I_{j}=1}^{D}v_{1,I_{1}}v_{2,I_{1}}\cdots v_{1,I_{j}}v_{2,I_{j}}=\sum_{I=1}^{D^{j}}v_{1,I}^{\otimes j}v_{2,I}^{\otimes j}.

By Lemma F.6 in
52
, there exists a ReLU feedforward network
\mathrm{Mult}
with
\mathcal{O}(\log(1/\epsilon_{\times})+\log C)
layers, width at most
96
,
\mathcal{O}(\log(1/\epsilon_{\times})+\log C)
non-zero neurons, and weight parameters upper bounded by
C^{2}
, such that

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Mult}({\mathcal{T}}_{1,I},{\mathcal{T}}_{2,I})-v_{1,I}^{\otimes j}v_{2,I}^{\otimes j}\right|\leq\epsilon_{\times}+2C\epsilon_{\text{error}},

which holds for any
I\in\{1,\ldots,D^{j}\}
. Therefore, taking

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{N}}_{\times}^{j}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2})=\sum_{I=1}^{D^{j}}\mathrm{Mult}({\mathcal{T}}_{1,I},{\mathcal{T}}_{2,I}),

we have

\displaystyle\left|{\mathfrak{N}}_{\times}^{j}({\mathcal{T}}_{1},{\mathcal{T}}_{2})-\left\langle v_{1}^{\otimes j},v_{2}^{\otimes j}\right\rangle\right|\leq\sum_{I=1}^{D^{j}}\left|\mathrm{Mult}({\mathcal{T}}_{1,I},{\mathcal{T}}_{2,I})-v_{1,I}^{\otimes j}v_{2,I}^{\otimes j}\right|\leq D^{j}\epsilon_{\times}+2CD^{j}\epsilon_{\text{error}}.

By Lemma F.1, F.2 and F.3 in
52
,
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\times}^{j}
can be exactly implemented by a ReLU feedforward network with
\mathcal{O}(\log(1/\epsilon_{\times})+\log C)
layers, width
\mathcal{O}(D^{j})
,
\mathcal{O}(D^{j}(\log(1/\epsilon_{\times})+\log C))
non-zero neurons, and weight parameters upper bounded by
C^{2}
.
∎

Lemma D.18

(Network Implementation for Projection Term)
.

Given any
j\in\{0,\ldots,\gamma-1\}
, and
\epsilon_{\rm proj}\in(0,1)
, there exists a tensor consisting of ReLU feedforward networks,
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm proj}^{j}=\{{\mathfrak{N}}^{j}_{\text{proj},I}\}_{I=1,2,\ldots,D^{j}}
, such that for any time
t\in[t_{0},T]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\alpha_{t}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{M},\tau)}\left|{\mathfrak{N}}^{j}_{\text{proj},I}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-\frac{[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]_{I}^{\otimes j}}{h_{t}^{j/2}}\right|\leq h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj}.

Here each network
{\mathfrak{N}}^{j}_{\text{proj},I}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\in\mathcal{F}(L,W,S,B,\cdot)
, where
L=c(\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}^{j/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj})))
,
W=S=c(\epsilon_{\rm proj})^{-d/\beta}\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}^{j/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj}))+\log^{4}(1/h_{t_{0}})
,
B=h_{t_{0}}^{-j}
, and the constant
c
depends on
D,d,\beta
,
B
and the smoothness of projection maps.

Proof of Lemma
D.18
.

Fix
I\in\{1,2,\ldots,D^{j}\}
. By Proposition
4.8
,
[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]_{I}^{\otimes j}
is
C^{\infty}
. Then we apply Lemma 2 in
12
with approximation error
h_{t}^{j/2}\epsilon_{\text{poly}}
and approximation degree

\widetilde{\beta}=\frac{D\beta\log(h_{t}^{(j+d)/2}\epsilon_{\text{poly}})}{d\log(\epsilon_{\text{poly}})}.

This gives that there exists a ReLU feedforward network,
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{N}}}_{\text{proj},I}^{j}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{{\mathfrak{N}}}^{j}_{\text{proj},I}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]_{I}^{\otimes j}\right|\leq h_{t}^{(j+d)/2}\epsilon_{\text{poly}},

which holds for any
x\in\alpha_{t}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{M},\tau)
and
\alpha_{t}\in[0,1]
. Here each network
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{N}}}^{j}_{\text{proj},I}
has no more than
c(\log(1/(h_{t}^{(j+d)/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj}))+1)
layers, and at most
