c^{\prime}(h_{t}^{(j+d)/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj})^{-D/\widetilde{\beta}}(\log(1/(h_{t}^{(j+d)/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj}))+1)
neurons and weight parameters, where the constant
c
depends on
D,d,\beta
,
B
and the smoothness of projection maps. Note that given the choice of
\widetilde{\beta}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(h_{t}^{(j+d)/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj}\right)^{-\frac{D}{\widetilde{\beta}}}=\left(h_{t}^{(j+d)/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj}\right)^{-\frac{d\log\left(\epsilon_{\text{poly}}\right)}{\beta\log\left(h_{t}^{(j+d)/2}\epsilon_{\text{poly}}\right)}}=\epsilon_{\rm proj}^{-\frac{d}{\beta}}.

Then by Lemma F.6-F.8 in
52
, there exists networks
{\mathfrak{N}}^{j}_{\text{proj},I}\in\mathcal{F}(L,W,S,B,\cdot)
such that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathfrak{N}}^{j}_{\text{proj},I}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-\frac{[x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}]_{I}^{\otimes j}}{h_{t}^{j/2}}\right|\leq h_{t}^{d/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj},

where the network configuration is
L=c(\log^{3}(1/(h_{t_{0}}^{j/2}\epsilon_{\rm proj})))
,
W=S=c(\epsilon_{\rm proj})^{-d/\beta}\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon_{\rm proj}))+\log^{4}(1/h_{t_{0}})
, and
B=h_{t_{0}}^{-j}
.
∎

Lemma D.19

(NN Implementation for Chart Determination)
.

Given
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
where
\|x\|_{\infty}\leq B_{\rm trunc}
, and functions
\{g_{k}:{\mathbb{R}^{D+2}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}}\}_{k=1,\ldots,C_{\mathcal{M}}}
, for any
\epsilon_{\rm{det}}\in(0,1)
, we can construct a network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}

such that for any
k\in[C_{\mathcal{M}}]
,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})=\begin{cases}1&\text{if}~\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}\leq(1-\epsilon_{\rm det})\bar{r}(t)^{2}\\
0&\text{if}~\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}\geq\bar{r}(t)^{2},\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})g_{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-\sum_{k:\|x_{k}-x\|\leq\bar{r}(t)}g_{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right\|\leq\left\|\sum_{k:(1-\epsilon_{\rm{det}})\bar{r}(t)<\|x_{k}-x\|<\bar{r}(t)}g_{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right\|.

Here
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}
is a network with number of layers, number of neurons and weight parameters bounded by
c\log(D(B+B_{\rm trunc})^{2}/(\epsilon_{\rm det}\bar{r}(t)^{2}))
, and width bounded by
cD
, where
c>0
is some absolute constant.

Proof of Lemma
D.19
.

We start with implementing the squared Euclidean distance. By Lemma 1 in
11
, there exists a network
\widehat{\sf sq}
that approximates the square function
{\sf sq}(x)=x^{2}
,
x\in[-1,1]
, with an arbitrary error
\epsilon_{\sf sq}>0
. The network c has a depth and the number of neurons and weight parameters no more than
c\log(1/\epsilon_{\sf sq})
for an absolute constant
c
, and the width of
\widehat{\sf sq}
is an absolute constant.

Now we utilize
\widehat{\sf sq}
to construct a network
\widehat{d}
implementing
\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}
. Given
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
where
\|x\|_{\infty}\leq B_{\rm trunc}
, we can write the distance as
\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{D}(B+B_{\rm trunc})^{2}\left(\frac{[x-x_{k}]_{i}}{B+B_{\rm trunc}}\right)^{2}
. Then we define

\displaystyle\widehat{d}(x-x_{k})=\sum_{i=1}^{D}(B+B_{\rm trunc})^{2}~\widehat{\sf sq}\left(\frac{[x-x_{k}]_{i}}{B+B_{\rm trunc}}\right).

Examining the summation in
\widehat{d}
, we note that
\widehat{d}
achieves the following approximation error

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{d}(x-x_{k})-\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}\right|\leq D(B+B_{\rm trunc})^{2}\epsilon_{\sf sq}.

Next, to implement
\widehat{\mathds{1}}
, we consider a basic step function
F(a)=2{\rm ReLU}(a-0.5\widetilde{r})-2{\rm ReLU}(a-\widetilde{r})
for some
\widetilde{r}>0
, which can be directly formulated as a feedforward layer. It is straightforward to check

\displaystyle F_{l}(a)=\underbrace{F\circ\cdots\circ F}_{l}(a)=\begin{cases}0&\text{if}~a<(1-2^{-l})\widetilde{r},\\
2^{l}(a-\widetilde{r})+\widetilde{r}&\text{if}~(1-2^{-l})\widetilde{r}\leq a\leq\widetilde{r}\\
1&\text{if}~a>\widetilde{r}\end{cases}.

Let
\widetilde{r}=(\bar{r}(t))^{2}-D(B+B_{\rm trunc})^{2}\epsilon_{\sf sq}
and
\epsilon_{\sf sq}=\epsilon_{\rm det}\bar{r}(t)^{2}/(2D(B+B_{\rm trunc})^{2})
. We choose
l
such that
(1-2^{-l})\widetilde{r}\geq(1-\epsilon_{\rm det})(\bar{r}(t))^{2}+D(B+B_{\rm trunc})^{2}\epsilon_{\sf sq}
, which yields
l=\lceil\log(1/\epsilon_{\rm det})\rceil
. Now take

\widehat{\mathds{1}}_{\epsilon_{\rm det}}=1-F_{l}(a)/\widetilde{r}
. Then the composition of
\widehat{\mathds{1}}_{\epsilon_{\rm det}}\circ\widehat{d}
verifies

\displaystyle\left[\widehat{\mathds{1}}_{\epsilon_{\rm det}}\circ\widehat{d}\right](x-x_{k})=\begin{cases}1&\text{if}~\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}\leq(1-\epsilon_{\rm det})\bar{r}(t)^{2}\\
0&\text{if}~\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}\geq\bar{r}(t)^{2}.\end{cases}

Hereby, we construct
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})=\left[\widehat{\mathds{1}}_{\epsilon_{\rm det}}\circ\widehat{d}\right](x-x_{k})
. The network implementation
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}
of the chart determination yields an error

\displaystyle\quad~\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})g_{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})-\sum_{k:\|x_{k}-x\|\leq\bar{r}(t)}g_{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\left({\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})-1\right)\mathds{1}\left((1-\epsilon_{\rm det})\bar{r}(t)^{2}<\|x-x_{k}\|^{2}<\bar{r}(t)^{2}\right)g_{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{k:(1-\epsilon_{\rm det})\bar{r}(t)<\|x_{k}-x\|<\bar{r}(t)}g_{k}(x,h_{t},\alpha_{t})\right\|.

Based on our construction,
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{det}}(x-x_{k})
has number of layers, width, and number of neurons and weight parameters bounded by
c\log(D(B+B_{\rm trunc})^{2}/(\epsilon_{\rm det}\bar{r}(t)^{2}))
for some absolute constant
c>0
.
∎

Lemma D.20

(Network Implementation for Polynomials)
.

Given any
k\in\{1,2,\ldots,C_{\mathcal{M}}\}
,
j\in\{0,\ldots,\gamma-1\}
, and
\epsilon_{\rm{poly}}\in(0,1)
, there exists a network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly}}^{k,j}=\{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly},I}^{k,j}\}_{I\in\{1,\ldots,D\}^{j}}
, such that for any
v\in[-C,C]^{d}
and time
t\in[t_{0},T]
,

\displaystyle\left|{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly},I}^{k,j}(v,h,\alpha)-\text{Tensor-Poly}_{I}^{k,j}(v,h,\alpha)\right|\leq h_{t_{0}}^{d/2}\epsilon_{\rm{poly}}.

Here each network
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\rm{poly},I}^{k,j}
has (i) no more than
c_{\rm{poly}}(\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon_{\rm{poly}}))+1)
layers with width bounded by
c_{\rm{poly}}(j+\gamma^{\prime}+1)^{d}
, and (ii) at most
c_{\rm{poly}}(j+\gamma^{\prime}+1)^{d}(\log(1/(h_{t_{0}}\epsilon_{\rm{poly}}))+1)
neurons and weight parameters bounded by
c_{\rm{poly}}C
, where the constant
c_{\rm{poly}}
depends on
d,\beta,C_{F}
and the smoothness of exponential maps
\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.20
.

By Lemma
D.16
,
\text{Tensor-Poly}_{I}^{k,j}(v,h,\alpha)
is a polynomial with degree at most
(\beta+1)(j+\gamma^{\prime}+1)
, and thus a Hölder function.
Then applying Theorem 3 in
11
directly yields the results.
∎

D.3
Helper Lemmas in Section
B.1.5

Lemma D.21
.

Let
\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)
and
\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)
satisfy that given
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
and
t
satisfying (
B.1
),

|\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)|\leq\epsilon_{1},~~\text{and}~~\|\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)-s_{2}(x,t)\|\leq\epsilon_{2}

for some sufficiently small
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
.
Then we have

\left\|s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\frac{\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}\right\|\leq\frac{C_{0}\sqrt{D}\alpha_{t}^{d}}{h_{t}^{(d+1)/2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{h_{t}}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}\exp\left(\frac{5(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}}\right)(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}),

where
C_{0}>0
is an absolute constant depending on
d,B_{\mathcal{M}},C_{f}
and
C_{G}
defined in Lemma
D.22
.

Proof.

By the definition of
s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)
given in (
B.4
), we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{h_{t}}\left\|s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\frac{\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}\right\|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{h_{t}}\left\|\frac{s_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{s_{1}(x,t)}-\frac{\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{s_{2}(x,t)}{s_{1}(x,t)}-\frac{s_{2}(x,t)}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}\right\|+\left\|\frac{s_{2}(x,t)}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}-\frac{\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|s_{2}(x,t)\|\cdot\|\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)-s_{1}(x,t)\|}{\|s_{1}(x,t)\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)\|}+\frac{\|\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)-s_{2}(x,t)\|}{\|\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)\|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|s_{2}(x,t)\|\epsilon_{1}}{\|s_{1}(x,t)\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)\|}+\frac{\epsilon_{2}}{\|\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)\|}.

Applying the lower bound on
s_{1}(x,t)
presented in Lemma
D.22
and the upper bound on
s_{2}(x,t)
in Lemma
D.23
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|s_{\mathcal{M}}(x,t)-\frac{\bar{s}_{2}(x,t)/\sqrt{h_{t}}}{\bar{s}_{1}(x,t)}\right\|\leq\frac{C_{0}\sqrt{D}\alpha_{t}^{d}}{h_{t}^{(d+1)/2}}\left(\log\frac{1}{h_{t}}\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}\exp\left(\frac{5(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}}\right)(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}),

where
C_{0}>0
is an absolute constant depending on
d,B_{\mathcal{M}},C_{f}
and
C_{G}
defined in Lemma
D.22
.
∎

Lemma D.22

(Lower Bound of
s_{1}(x,t)
)
.

Given time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(1/36+\tau^{2})
, and
x\in\mathcal{B}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},\alpha_{t}\tau)
, we have

s_{1}(x,t)\geq C_{f}^{-1}C_{G}^{-1}\frac{(\pi h_{t})^{d/2}e^{-1/8}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)(2\alpha_{t})^{d}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{2\tau\alpha_{t}}\right).

Here
C_{G}>0
is a constant such that
C_{G}^{-1}\leq|G_{k}(v)|\leq C_{G}
for all
v\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)
and
k=1,\ldots,C_{\mathcal{M}}
.

Proof.

Recall the definition of
s_{1}(x,t)
:

\displaystyle s_{1}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}+2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\rho_{k}(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v))\cdot p_{\rm data}(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v))\cdot G_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

By Assumption
4.3
,
p_{\rm data}
is lower bounded by
C_{f}^{-1}
. Moreover, supposing
\min_{v\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)}G_{k}(v)\geq C_{G}^{-1}
for any
k=1,\ldots,C_{\mathcal{M}}
, we can derive

\displaystyle s_{1}(x,t)\geq C_{f}^{-1}C_{G}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)}

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\left(\frac{2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Noting that the integrand is non-negative, we can shrink the integral region as follows:

\displaystyle s_{1}(x,t)\geq C_{f}^{-1}C_{G}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{h_{t}}}{2\alpha_{t}}}

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\left(\frac{2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

When the time
t
satisfies
h_{t}\leq\tau^{2}/(1/36+\tau^{2})
, we have
\sqrt{h_{t}}/(2\alpha_{t})\leq 3\tau
. Then given
\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{h_{t}}}{2\alpha_{t}}
, we can apply Lemma
D.6
to get

\displaystyle-\frac{\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{h_{t}}\geq-\frac{2\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|\cdot\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{\tau\alpha_{t}h_{t}}\geq-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{2\tau\alpha_{t}}.

This further yields

\displaystyle s_{1}(x,t)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{h_{t}}}{2\alpha_{t}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{8}-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{2\tau\alpha_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)}\left(\frac{\sqrt{h_{t}}}{2\alpha_{t}}\right)^{d}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{8}-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{2\tau\alpha_{t}}\right).

Here in the last inequality we use
\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\rho_{k}=1
. In conclusion, we obtain the following lower bound for
s_{1}(x,t)
:

\displaystyle s_{1}(x,t)\geq C_{f}^{-1}C_{G}^{-1}\frac{(\pi h_{t})^{d/2}e^{-1/8}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)(2\alpha_{t})^{d}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{2\tau\alpha_{t}}\right).

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma D.23

(Upper Bound of
s_{2}(x,t)
)
.

Given time
t
satisfying
h_{t}\leq\min\{\tau^{2}/(64DL_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}}^{2}\log(1/\delta)/9+\tau^{2}),\tau^{2}/(4c_{0}^{2}((d+1)\log(1/h_{t})/2)/L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}^{2}+\tau^{2})\}
, and
x\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha_{t}\mathcal{M},2\sqrt{Dh_{t}\log(1/\delta)})
, we have

\|s_{2}(x,t)\|\leq\left[\frac{C_{f}C_{G}\pi^{d/2}\left(2(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)\alpha_{t}^{d}}\exp\left(\frac{4(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})\cdot\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}}\right)+\sqrt{D}B\right]h_{t}^{d/2}.

Proof.

Recall the definition of
s_{2}(x,t)
:

\displaystyle s_{2}(x,t)=\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)}

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}(x^{*}_{t}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v))\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\left(-\frac{2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Notice that we have
\sup_{x,y\in\mathcal{M}}\|x-y\|\leq\sqrt{D}B
according to Assumption
4.1
. Then by Lemma
D.4
, taking
\epsilon_{1}=\sqrt{h_{t}}/\alpha_{t}
and
\widetilde{\Delta}=\sqrt{2(d+1)h_{t}\log(1/h_{t})/\alpha_{t}^{2}}
yields

\displaystyle\|s_{2}(x,t)\|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\widetilde{\Delta}}

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\|x^{*}_{t}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|\exp\left(-\frac{\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v+\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}^{d/2},

Next, we bound the cross term by Lemma
D.6
:

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\widetilde{\Delta}}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\|x^{*}_{t}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|\exp\left(-\frac{2\langle x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t},\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\rangle}{2h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\widetilde{\Delta}}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\|x^{*}_{t}-\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|\exp\left(\frac{2\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|\cdot\|\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}-\alpha_{t}\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)\|^{2}}{\tau\alpha_{t}h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\widetilde{\Delta}}\frac{\alpha_{t}\widetilde{\Delta}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\exp\left(\frac{2\alpha_{t}^{2}\widetilde{\Delta}^{2}\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v.

Since
p_{\rm data}
is upper bounded by
C_{f}
(Assumption
4.3
) and
\sup_{v\in\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,r)}G_{k}(v)\leq C_{G}
for any
k=1,\ldots,C_{\mathcal{M}}
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\widetilde{\Delta}}\frac{\alpha_{t}\widetilde{\Delta}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\exp\left(\frac{2\alpha_{t}^{2}\widetilde{\Delta}^{2}\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}h_{t}}\right)F_{k}(v){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{C_{\mathcal{M}}}\int_{\|\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)-x^{*}_{t}\|\leq\widetilde{\Delta}}C_{f}C_{G}\frac{\alpha_{t}\widetilde{\Delta}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\exp\left(\frac{2\alpha_{t}^{2}\widetilde{\Delta}^{2}\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(v)){\,\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle\leq C_{f}C_{G}\frac{\pi^{d/2}\widetilde{\Delta}^{d}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{t}\widetilde{\Delta}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\cdot\exp\left(\frac{2\alpha_{t}^{2}\widetilde{\Delta}^{2}\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}h_{t}}\right).

Putting together the above inequalities leads to

\displaystyle\|s_{2}(x,t)\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{f}C_{G}\frac{\pi^{d/2}\widetilde{\Delta}^{d}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{t}\widetilde{\Delta}}{\sqrt{h_{t}}}\cdot\exp\left(\frac{2\alpha_{t}^{2}\widetilde{\Delta}^{2}\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}h_{t}}\right)+\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}^{d/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{f}C_{G}(\pi h_{t})^{d/2}\left(2(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})\right)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)\alpha_{t}^{d}}\exp\left(\frac{4(d+1)\log(1/h_{t})\cdot\|x-\alpha_{t}x^{*}_{t}\|}{\tau\alpha_{t}}\right)+\sqrt{D}Bh_{t}^{d/2}.

The proof is complete.
∎

D.4
Helper Lemmas in Section
B.2

Lemma D.24
.

For any
\epsilon,\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
and time
t>0
satisfying
h_{t}\geq\epsilon^{2/\beta}/4
. Let
\bar{r}(t)=2\sqrt{h_{t}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+h_{t}B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon^{1/\beta}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k:\|x-x_{k}\|>\bar{r}(t)}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})\right|\leq\epsilon_{0}.

Proof.

Recall the atlas
\{(U_{k},\mathrm{\mathbb{Log}}_{k})\}_{k=1}^{\mathcal{M}}
where
U_{k}=\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,\epsilon^{1/\beta})
. It satisfies
U_{k}\subseteq\mathcal{B}^{D}(x_{k},L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon^{1/\beta})
. For the
k
-th chart satisfying
\|x-x_{k}\|>\bar{r}(t)
and any
x_{0}\in U_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|

\displaystyle\geq\|x-x_{k}\|-\|x_{k}-\alpha_{t}x_{k}\|-\|\alpha_{t}x_{k}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|

\displaystyle\geq\bar{r}(t)-(1-\alpha_{t})B-\alpha_{t}L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon^{1/\beta}

\displaystyle\geq\bar{r}(t)-h_{t}B-L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon^{1/\beta},

where the last inequality uses
1-\alpha_{t}=(1-\alpha_{t}^{2})/(1+\alpha_{t})=h_{t}/(1+\alpha_{t})\leq h_{t}
. Since we take
\bar{r}(t)=2\sqrt{h_{t}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+h_{t}B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon^{1/\beta}
, we have

\displaystyle\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|\geq 2\sqrt{h_{t}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}.

This further yields

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{4h_{t}\log(1/\epsilon)}{2h_{t}}\right)\leq\epsilon_{0}.

Therefore, we can conclude

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{k:\|x-x_{k}\|>\bar{r}(t)}\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\|^{2}}{2h_{t}}\right)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0})\right|\leq\epsilon_{0}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma D.25
.

For any
\epsilon,\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
, and time
t>0
satisfying
h_{t}\geq\epsilon^{2/\beta}/4
. Let
\bar{r}(t)=2\sqrt{h_{t}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+h_{t}B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon^{1/\beta}
, and
S=4e^{2}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})+4e^{2}(B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}+1)^{2}
. Then for
\text{Poly}^{k}
defined in (
B.32
) with any
\gamma_{0}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{K}_{t}(\epsilon_{0})}\left|\text{Poly}^{k}(x,t)-I_{k}(x,t)\right|=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+B)^{\gamma_{0}}}{\tau^{\gamma_{0}}}\epsilon^{\gamma_{0}/\beta}+\epsilon_{0}\right),

Proof.

We begin with approximating the exponential functions that take
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)
and
\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)
as inputs. By the definition of
\Pi_{k}(x,t)
, we have
x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\perp\alpha_{t}\cdot T_{x_{k}}\mathcal{M}
, which yields

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)=\langle x-\Pi_{k}(x,t),\Pi_{k}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{k}+\alpha_{t}x_{k}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle=\langle x-\Pi_{k}(x,t),\alpha_{t}x_{k}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle.

Then by Lemma
D.6
, for any
x_{0}\in U_{k}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\langle x-\Pi_{k}(x,t),\alpha_{t}x_{k}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle\right|\leq\frac{2\alpha_{t}}{\tau}\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|\cdot\|x_{k}-x_{0}\|^{2}\leq\frac{2\alpha_{t}\epsilon^{2/\beta}}{\tau}\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|.

For the selected charts satisfying
\|x-x_{k}\|\leq\bar{r}(t)
, we can derive

\displaystyle\|x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\|\leq\|x-\alpha_{t}x_{k}\|\leq\|x-x_{k}\|+\|x_{k}-\alpha_{t}x_{k}\|\leq\bar{r}(t)+h_{t}B.

Plugging in
\bar{r}(t)=2\sqrt{h_{t}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+h_{t}B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon^{1/\beta}
, we can derive

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)\right|\leq\frac{2\alpha_{t}\epsilon^{2/\beta}(2\sqrt{h_{t}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+2h_{t}B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon^{1/\beta})}{h_{t}\tau}\leq\frac{8(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})\epsilon^{1/\beta}}{\tau},

where we apply
h_{t}\geq\epsilon^{2/\beta}/4
in the last inequality.
Now we approximate the exponential function with a polynomial with degree
\gamma_{0}
.
By Lemma
E.3
, the approximation error can be bounded as

\displaystyle\left|\exp\left(-\frac{1}{h_{t}}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)\right)-\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma_{0}-1}\frac{(-1)^{l}\alpha_{t}^{l}}{h_{t}^{l}l!}{\mathcal{T}}^{l}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)\right|\leq\left(\frac{8(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}})\epsilon^{1/\beta}}{\tau}\right)^{\gamma_{0}}.

(D.16)

Likewise, we can derive the upper bound for
\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)
. For any
x_{0}\in U_{k}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2h_{t}}\left(\|\Pi_{k}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{k}\|+\alpha_{t}^{2}\|x_{k}-x_{0}\|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2h_{t}}\left(\|x-x_{k}\|+\|x_{k}-\alpha_{t}x_{k}\|+\alpha_{t}^{2}\|x_{k}-x_{0}\|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2h_{t}}\left(\bar{r}(t)+h_{t}B+\epsilon^{1/\beta}\right)^{2}.

Utilizing
\bar{r}(t)=2\sqrt{h_{t}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+h_{t}B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}\epsilon^{1/\beta}
and
h_{t}\geq\epsilon^{2/\beta}/4
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}+1\right)^{2}.

Then we can derive the following polynomial approximation result. Set
S=4e^{2}\log(1/\epsilon_{0})+4e^{2}(B+L_{\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}}+1)^{2}
. Lemma
E.3
shows that

\displaystyle\left|\exp\left(-\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}}{2h_{t}}\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)\right)-\sum_{j=0}^{S-1}\frac{(-1)^{j}\alpha_{t}^{2j}}{2^{j}h_{t}^{j}j!}\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)^{2j}\right|\leq\epsilon_{0}.

(D.17)

Finally, combining the approximation results in (
D.16
) and (
D.17
), we can derive the approximation error of
\text{Poly}^{k}
to
I_{k}
. Since
\text{Poly}^{k}
and
I_{k}
are integrals over
U_{k}=\mathrm{\mathbb{Exp}}_{k}(\mathcal{B}^{d}(0,\epsilon^{1/\beta}))
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\text{Poly}^{k}(x,t)-I_{k}(x,t)\right|=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{(\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon_{0})}+B)^{\gamma_{0}}}{\tau^{\gamma_{0}}}\epsilon^{(d+\gamma_{0})/\beta}+\epsilon^{d/\beta}\epsilon_{0}\right),

which holds for any chart
k\in[C_{\mathcal{M}}]
.
∎

Lemma D.26
.

For any
k=1,\ldots,C_{\mathcal{M}}
, let
P_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times d}
be a matrix with columns forming an orthonormal basis of
T_{x_{k}}\mathcal{M}
. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
and
t>0
,
\text{Poly}^{k}(x,t)
defined in (
B.32
) is a polynomial with respect to
x
up to order
\gamma_{0}
, and
P_{k}^{\top}x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
up to order
S
, with the form

\displaystyle\text{Poly}^{k}(x,t)=\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=0}^{S-1}\frac{1}{h_{t}^{l+j}}\sum_{p=0}^{2(l+j)}\alpha_{t}^{p}\sum_{|\theta|\leq l,|\gamma|\leq j}a_{l,j,p,\theta,\xi}x^{\theta}(P_{k}^{\top}x)^{\xi}.

Here
\{a_{l,j,p,\theta,\xi}\}
are constants in
\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

Recall the definition of
\text{Poly}^{k}(x,t)
in (
B.32
)

\displaystyle\text{Poly}^{k}(x,t):=

\displaystyle\int_{x_{0}\in U_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma_{0}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{S-1}\frac{(-1)^{l+j}\alpha_{t}^{l+2j}}{2^{j}h_{t}^{l+j}l!j!}{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{l}(x,x_{0},t)\mathcal{D}_{k}^{j}(x,x_{0},t)\rho_{k}(x_{0})p_{\rm data}(x_{0}){\,\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(x_{0}),

where by definition, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)=\langle x-\Pi_{k}(x,t),\Pi_{k}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)=\|\Pi_{k}(x,t)/\alpha_{t}-x_{0}\|^{2}.

Since
x-\Pi_{k}(x,t)\perp\alpha_{t}\cdot T_{x_{k}}\mathcal{M}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)=\langle x-\Pi_{k}(x,t),\Pi_{k}(x,t)-\alpha_{t}x_{k}+\alpha_{t}x_{k}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle=\langle x-\Pi_{k}(x,t),\alpha_{t}x_{k}-\alpha_{t}x_{0}\rangle.

Note that the projection
\Pi_{k}
admits a linear formulation.
By definition, the columns of
P_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times d}
form an orthonormal basis of
T_{x_{k}}\mathcal{M}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{k}(x,t)=P_{k}P_{k}^{\top}(x-\alpha_{t}x_{k})+\alpha_{t}x_{k}.

(D.18)

Therefore, we can rewrite
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)
and
\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)
as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)=(x_{k}-x_{0})^{\top}\left(I-P_{k}P_{k}^{\top}\right)(x-\alpha_{t}x_{k}),

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)=\left\|\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}}P_{k}P_{k}^{\top}(x-\alpha_{t}x_{k})+(x_{k}-x_{0})\right\|^{2}.

Notably, given
x_{0}\in U_{k}
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)
is linear in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
, while
\mathcal{D}_{k}(x,x_{0},t)
is quadratic in the low-dimensional representation
P_{k}^{\top}(x-\alpha_{t}x_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Since the integral region
U_{k}
in
\text{Poly}^{k}(x;t)
is independent of
x
,
\text{Poly}^{k}(x;t)
after performing the integration is a polynomial with the form

\displaystyle\text{Poly}^{k}(x;t)=\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=0}^{S-1}\frac{1}{h_{t}^{l+j}}\sum_{p=0}^{2(l+j)}\alpha_{t}^{p}\sum_{|\theta|\leq l,|\gamma|\leq j}a_{l,j,p,\theta,\xi}x^{\theta}(P_{k}^{\top}x)^{\xi},

where
\{a_{l,j,p,\theta,\xi}\}
are constants in
\mathbb{R}
.
We conclude that
\text{Poly}^{k}(x;t)
is a polynomial with respect to
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
up to order
\gamma_{0}
, and
P_{k}^{\top}x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
up to order
S
.
∎

Appendix E
Auxiliary Lemmas

Lemma E.1

(Jacobian matrix of the projection function)
.

Given a manifold
\mathcal{M}
with reach
r
, for any
x\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{M},\tau)
, let
\Pi(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{y\in\mathcal{M}}\|x-y\|
. We have

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\Pi(x)(x-\Pi(x))=0

Proof.

Firstly, we show that
\Pi(x+\Delta x)-\Pi(x)=0
, where
\Delta x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
is a small perturbation of
x
belonging to the normal space of
\mathcal{M}
at
\Pi(x)
, and
x,x+\Delta x\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{M},\tau)
. By definition, we have

\displaystyle\|(x+\Delta x)-\Pi(x+\Delta x)\|=\min_{y\in\mathcal{M}}\|(x+\Delta x)-y\|.

Here we decompose
y-\Pi(x)
, for any
y\in\mathcal{M}
, as
y-\Pi(x)=u+v
, where
u\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
lies in the tangent space of
\mathcal{M}
at
\Pi(x)
, and
v\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
lies in the normal space of
\mathcal{M}
at
\Pi(x)
. Due to orthogonality, we have

\displaystyle\min_{y\in\mathcal{M}}\|(x+\Delta x)-y\|

\displaystyle=\min_{\Pi(x)+u+v\in\mathcal{M}}\|(x+\Delta x)-(\Pi(x)+u+v)\|

\displaystyle=\min_{\Pi(x)+u+v\in\mathcal{M}}\|x-\Pi(x)+\Delta x-v\|+\|u\|

\displaystyle=\min_{\Pi(x)+v\in\mathcal{M}}\|x-\Pi(x)+\Delta x-v\|.

Note that
\Pi(x)+v\in\mathcal{M}
if and only if
v=0
, since
v
is in the normal space of
\mathcal{M}
at
\Pi(x)\in\mathcal{M}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|(x+\Delta x)-\Pi(x+\Delta x)\|=\|x-\Pi(x)+\Delta x\|,

and thus
\Pi(x+\Delta x)=\Pi(x)
. This yields
\nabla_{x}\Pi(x)\Delta x=0
. Taking
\Delta x=-x+\Pi(x)
concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma E.2

(High probability region of Gaussian Samples)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a manifold in
\mathbb{R}^{D}
and fix any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma>0
. For each point
x\in\mathcal{M}
, we perturb it as
x^{\prime}=\alpha x+\sigma\xi
, where
\xi\sim{\sf N}(0,I_{D})
. Given an arbitrary
\delta\in(0,e^{-2})
, drawing
x^{\prime}
randomly via sampling
x\sim P_{\rm data}
on the manifold and
\xi\sim{\sf N}(0,\sigma^{2}I_{D})
independently satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha\mathcal{M},2\sigma\sqrt{D\log(1/\delta)}\right)\geq 1-\delta^{D}.

Proof.

Due to the sampling of
x^{\prime}
, for any
R>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(x^{\prime}\not\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha\mathcal{M},R)\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\int_{x\in\mathcal{M}}\mathbb{P}_{x^{\prime}\sim{\sf N}(\alpha x,\sigma^{2}I_{D})}\left(x^{\prime}\not\in\mathcal{B}^{D}(\alpha x,R)\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{x\in\mathcal{M}}\mathbb{P}_{x^{\prime}\sim{\sf N}(\alpha x,\sigma^{2}I_{D})}\left(\|x^{\prime}-\alpha x\|>R\right){\,\mathrm{d}}P_{\rm data}(x).

where inequality
(i)
follows from the union bound. Plugging in
R=2\sigma\sqrt{D\log\frac{1}{\delta}}
, we only need to verify

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{x^{\prime}\sim{\sf N}(x,\sigma^{2}I_{D})}\left(\|x^{\prime}-\alpha x\|>2\sigma\sqrt{D\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)\leq\delta^{D}.

(E.1)

To verify inequality (
E.1
), we note that
(x^{\prime}-\alpha x)/\sigma\sim{\sf N}(0,I_{D})
. Therefore, it suffices to show

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{z\sim{\sf N}(0,I_{D})}\left(\|z\|>2\sqrt{D\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)\leq\delta^{D}.

In fact, we observe that
\|z\|^{2}
follows from the
\chi^{2}
-square distribution with freedom
D
. Hence, invoking
38
, for any
u>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{z\sim{\sf N}(0,I_{D})}\left(\|z\|^{2}>D+2\sqrt{Du}+2u\right)\leq\exp(-u).

Setting
u=D\log\frac{1}{\delta}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{z\sim{\sf N}(0,I_{D})}\left(\|z\|^{2}>D+2D\sqrt{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}+2D\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\leq\delta^{D}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow~

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{z\sim{\sf N}(0,I_{D})}\left(\|z\|^{2}>4D\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\leq\delta^{D},

since
4\log\frac{1}{\delta}>2\log\frac{1}{\delta}+2\sqrt{\log\frac{1}{\delta}}+1
when
0<\delta<e^{-2}
. Thus, inequality (
E.1
) is verified. The proof is complete by considering
\mathbb{P}\left(x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha\mathcal{M},R)\right)=1-\mathbb{P}\left(x^{\prime}\not\in\mathcal{K}(\alpha\mathcal{M},R)\right)
.
∎

Lemma E.3

(Approximating a smooth univariate function with Taylor polynomials)
.

Let
A>0
,
\epsilon>0
and
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function with partial derivatives up to
\mathcal{S}_{0}
-th order bounded by some constant
\eta>0
. Choose
{\mathcal{S}}\in[\max\{e^{2}A,\log(\eta/\epsilon)\},\mathcal{S}_{0}]
. Then we have

\bigg|f(x)-\sum_{|\theta|<{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{\partial^{\theta}f(0)}{\theta!}x^{\theta}\bigg|\leq\epsilon,

which holds for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
\|x\|\leq A
.

Proof.

By the Taylor’s theorem for multivariate functions, we have

\displaystyle f(x)=\sum_{\theta<{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{\partial^{\theta}f(0)}{\theta!}x^{\theta}+\frac{h_{\mathcal{S}}(x)}{{\mathcal{S}}!}x^{\mathcal{S}},

where
|h_{\mathcal{S}}(x)|\leq\max_{y}|\partial^{\mathcal{S}}f(y)|\leq\eta
. Thereby it follows that

\displaystyle\bigg|f(x)-\sum_{\theta<{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{\partial^{\theta}f(0)}{\theta!}x^{\theta}\bigg|\leq\frac{\eta}{{\mathcal{S}}!}|x^{\mathcal{S}}|.

Using
{\mathcal{S}}!\geq({\mathcal{S}}/e)^{\mathcal{S}}
, we further derive that

\displaystyle\bigg|f(x)-\sum_{|\theta|<{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{\partial^{\theta}f(0)}{\theta!}x^{\theta}\bigg|\leq\frac{\eta A^{\mathcal{S}}}{({\mathcal{S}}/e)^{\mathcal{S}}}=\eta\left(\frac{eA}{{\mathcal{S}}}\right)^{\mathcal{S}}.

Since we choose
{\mathcal{S}}
such that
{\mathcal{S}}\geq\max\{e^{2}A,\log(\eta/\epsilon)\}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\bigg|f(x)-\sum_{|\theta|<{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{\partial^{\theta}f(0)}{\theta!}x^{\theta}\bigg|\leq\eta\left(\frac{eA}{e^{2}A}\right)^{\mathcal{S}}=\eta e^{-{\mathcal{S}}}\leq\epsilon.

The proof is complete.
∎

Appendix F
Average Taylor Polynomial

In this section, we provide the definition and properties of average Taylor polynomials.

Definition F.1

(Averaged Taylor polynomials)
.

Suppose
f\in C^{\alpha-1}(\Omega)
where
\mathcal{B}(z_{0},\rho)\subseteq\Omega
. The corresponding average Taylor polynomial of degree
\alpha
of
f
averaged over
\mathcal{B}(z_{0},\rho)
is defined as

\displaystyle Q_{z_{0}}^{\alpha}f(x)=\int_{\mathcal{B}(z_{0},\rho)}T_{z}^{\alpha}f(x)\phi(z)dz

(F.1)

with

\displaystyle T_{z}^{\alpha}f(x)=\sum_{|\theta|<\alpha}\frac{\partial^{\theta}f(z)}{|\theta|!}(x-z)^{\theta}.

(F.2)

Here
\phi
being arbitrary cut-off function satisfying

\displaystyle\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\mbox{ with }\phi(z)\geq 0\mbox{ for all }z\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

\displaystyle{\rm supp}(\phi)=\overline{\mathcal{B}(z_{0},\rho)}\mbox{ and }\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}\phi(z)dz=1.

For
f\in C^{\alpha}(\Omega)
, we define its Sobolev norm
[
8
, Definition 1.3.1]
as
\|f\|_{W^{\alpha,\infty}(\Omega)}=\max_{|\theta|\leq\alpha}\|\partial^{\theta}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}
with
\theta
a multi-index.
The averaged Taylor polynomial can approximate
f
and its partial derivatives well. Specifically, Lemma
F.2
provides an approximation guarantee in Sobolev norm.

Lemma F.2

(Bramble-Hilbert, Chapter 4.1 in
8
)
.

Suppose
f\in C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{B}(z_{0},\rho))
. There exists a constant
C_{\alpha,d}>0
such that

\left\|f-Q^{\alpha}_{z_{0}}f\right\|_{W^{p,\infty}(\mathcal{B}(z_{0},\rho))}\leq C_{\alpha,d}\rho^{\alpha-p}\|f\|_{W^{\alpha,\infty}(\mathcal{B}(z_{0},\rho))}~~\text{ for }p=0,1,\ldots,\alpha,

where
Q^{\alpha}_{z_{0}}f
denotes the averaged Taylor polynomial of degree
\alpha
of
f
averaged over
\mathcal{B}(z_{0},\rho)
.

Lemma
F.3
below shows that
Q_{z_{0}}^{\alpha}f
can be written as a weighted sum of polynomials.

Lemma F.3

(Proposition 4.1.12 of
8
)
.

Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
f\in W^{\alpha-1,\infty}(\Omega)
. Let
z_{0}\in\Omega,\rho>0
such that
\mathcal{B}(z_{0},\rho)\subseteq\Omega
. Then the averaged Taylor polynomial
Q_{z_{0}}^{\alpha}(f)
can be written as

\displaystyle Q_{z_{0}}^{\alpha}f(x)=\sum_{|\theta|<\alpha}c_{\theta}x^{\theta},\quad\text{for }x\in\Omega.

(F.3)

Moreover, there exists a constant
C
depending on
\alpha,d,\rho
such that for all
|\theta|<\alpha
,

|c_{\theta}|\leq C\|f\|_{W^{\alpha-1,\infty}}(\Omega).

Appendix G
Polynomial Size

The core of our approximation theory depends on approximating smooth functions with Taylor expansions. In this section, we develop auxiliary lemmas to control the size of the polynomials.

We see that a function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
can be approximated well by its Taylor polynomial

\widehat{f}(x)=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq d_{f}}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}\frac{\partial^{\alpha}f(x_{0})}{|\alpha|!}(x-x_{0})^{\alpha},

where
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{d})
with
\alpha_{i}\geq 0
is a multi-index,
|\alpha|=\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}
, and

x^{\alpha}=\prod_{i}(x_{i})^{\alpha_{i}},\partial^{\alpha}=(\partial_{1})^{\alpha_{1}}\cdots(\partial_{d})^{\alpha_{d}}.

The combinatorial number
{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}
is

{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}=\frac{|\alpha|!}{\alpha_{1}!\cdots\alpha_{d}!}

For
\alpha+\beta=\gamma
, define

{\gamma\choose\alpha\quad\beta}=\prod_{i}{\gamma_{i}\choose\alpha_{i}\quad\beta_{i}}=\prod_{i}\frac{\gamma_{i}!}{\alpha_{i}!\beta_{i}!}

Lemma G.1
.

We have the following bound

{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}\frac{2^{|\alpha|}}{|\alpha|!}\leq 2^{d}.

Lemma G.2

(Multiplication)
.

Assume we have two polynomial approximations
\widehat{f}
and
\widehat{g}
, both centered at
x_{0}
:

\widehat{f}(x)=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq d_{f}}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}\frac{\partial^{\alpha}f(x_{0})}{|\alpha|!}(x-x_{0})^{\alpha},

and

\widehat{g}(x)=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq d_{g}}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}\frac{\partial^{\alpha}g(x_{0})}{|\alpha|!}(x-x_{0})^{\alpha}.

Assume that there exist constants
\{C_{f,\alpha},C_{g,\alpha},C_{\alpha}\}
, such that
|{\partial^{\alpha}f(x_{0})}|\leq C_{f,\alpha}
and
|{\partial^{\alpha}g(x_{0})}|\leq C_{g,\alpha}
, and
\sup_{\alpha+\beta=\gamma}C_{f,\alpha}C_{g,\beta}\leq C_{\gamma}
.

We have that the coefficient of the
(x-x_{0})^{\gamma}
term of
\widehat{f}(x)\widehat{g}(x)
is bounded by

{|\gamma|\choose\gamma}\frac{2^{|\gamma|}\cdot C_{\gamma}}{|\gamma|!}

The degree of
\widehat{f}(x)\widehat{g}(x)
is at most
d_{f}+d_{g}
.

Lemma G.3

(Exponentiation)
.

Assume we a polynomial approximation
\widehat{f}
centered at
x_{0}
:

\widehat{f}(x)=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq d_{f}}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}\frac{\partial^{\alpha}f(x_{0})}{|\alpha|!}(x-x_{0})^{\alpha}.

Assume that
\sup|{\partial^{\alpha}f(x_{0})}|\leq 2^{|\alpha|}C_{f}
. Let
\widehat{g}(z)
be the
d_{g}
-th order Talyor expansion of the exponential function,

\widehat{g}(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{d_{g}}\frac{1}{k!}z^{k}

The coefficient of the
(x-x_{0})^{\gamma}
term of
\widehat{g}(\widehat{f}(x))
is bounded by

{|\gamma|\choose\gamma}\frac{1}{|\gamma|!}\cdot{\exp(2^{|\gamma|}C_{f})}

The degree of
\widehat{g}(\widehat{f}(x))
is at most
d_{f}d_{g}
.

Lemma G.4

(Addition)
.

Assume we a polynomial approximation
\widehat{f}
centered at
x_{0}
:

\widehat{f}(x)=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq d_{f}}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}\frac{\partial^{\alpha}f(x_{0})}{|\alpha|!}(x-x_{0})^{\alpha}.

Consider
g(u,v)=\widehat{f}(u+v)
, we have

g(u,v)=\sum_{|(\alpha,\beta)|\leq d_{f}}{|\alpha,\beta|\choose\alpha,\beta}\frac{\partial^{(\alpha+\beta)}f(x_{0})}{|\alpha,\beta|!}(u-u_{0})^{\alpha}(v-v_{0})^{\beta},

for any pair of
(u_{0},v_{0})
satisfying
u_{0}+v_{0}=x_{0}
, where
(\alpha,\beta)
is a multi-index for the
2d
input
(u,v)
.

Lemma G.5

(Partial Integral)
.

Assume we a polynomial approximation
\widehat{g}
centered at
(u_{0},v_{0})
:

\widehat{g}(u,v)=\sum_{|(\alpha,\beta)|\leq d_{g}}{|\alpha,\beta|\choose\alpha,\beta}\frac{\partial^{(\alpha,\beta)}g(u_{0},v_{0})}{|\alpha,\beta|!}(u-u_{0})^{\alpha}(v-v_{0})^{\beta},

where we assume that there exists
C_{g,\beta}
such that
\sup_{\alpha}|{\partial^{(\alpha,\beta)}g(u_{0},v_{0})}|\leq C_{g,\beta}
.
Define

f(v)=\int_{u\in U}\widehat{g}(u,v)\mathrm{d}u.

The coefficient of the
(v-v_{0})^{\beta}
term of
f
is bounded by

{|\beta|\choose\beta}\frac{C_{g,\beta}}{|\beta|!}\cdot 2^{d}\exp(Bd),

where
B=\sup_{u\in U}\|u-u_{0}\|_{\infty}
.

G.1
Proofs for the lemmas

proof of Lemma
G.2
.

Let’s focus on the
(x-x_{0})^{\gamma}
term of
\widehat{f}(x)\widehat{g}(x)
. The coefficient of
(x-x_{0})^{\gamma}
is

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq d_{f},|\beta|\leq d_{g},\alpha+\beta=\gamma}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}\frac{\partial^{\alpha}f(x_{0})}{|\alpha|!}{|\beta|\choose\beta}\frac{\partial^{\beta}g(x_{0})}{|\beta|!},

whose absolute value can be bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq d_{f},|\beta|\leq d_{g},\alpha+\beta=\gamma}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}\frac{1}{|\alpha|!}{|\beta|\choose\beta}\frac{1}{|\beta|!}C_{f,\alpha}C_{g,\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq d_{f},|\beta|\leq d_{g},\alpha+\beta=\gamma}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}\frac{1}{|\alpha|!}{|\beta|\choose\beta}\frac{1}{|\beta|!}C_{\gamma}

(Using the assumption that
\sup_{\alpha+\beta=\gamma}C_{f,\alpha}C_{g,\beta}\leq C_{\gamma}
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{|\alpha|\leq d_{f},|\beta|\leq d_{g},\alpha+\beta=\gamma}\frac{|\gamma|!}{|\alpha|!|\beta|!}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}{|\beta|\choose\beta}\frac{1}{|\gamma|!}C_{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha+\beta=\gamma}\frac{|\gamma|!}{|\alpha|!|\beta|!}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}{|\beta|\choose\beta}\frac{1}{|\gamma|!}C_{\gamma}

(relaxing the
|\alpha|\leq d_{f},|\beta|\leq d_{g}
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha+\beta=\gamma}\frac{|\gamma|!}{(\alpha_{1}!\beta_{1}!)\cdots(\alpha_{d}!\beta_{d}!)}\frac{1}{|\gamma|!}C_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha+\beta=\gamma}\frac{\gamma_{1}!\cdots\gamma_{d}!}{(\alpha_{1}!\beta_{1}!)\cdots(\alpha_{d}!\beta_{d}!)}\frac{|\gamma|!}{\gamma_{1}!\cdots\gamma_{d}!}\frac{1}{|\gamma|!}C_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{\gamma_{1}+\dots\gamma_{d}}\frac{|\gamma|!}{\gamma_{1}!\cdots\gamma_{d}!}\frac{1}{|\gamma|!}C_{\gamma}.

(Applying the combinatorial equality
\sum_{\alpha_{i}+\beta_{i}=\gamma_{i}}\gamma_{i}!/\alpha_{i}!\beta_{i}!=2^{\gamma_{i}}
)

∎

proof of Lemma
G.3
.

Note that we have

\widehat{g}(\widehat{f}(x))=\sum_{k=0}^{d_{g}}\frac{1}{k!}\Big(\sum_{|\alpha|\leq d_{f}}{|\alpha|\choose\alpha}\frac{\partial^{\alpha}f(x_{0})}{|\alpha|!}(x-x_{0})^{\alpha}\Big)^{k}.

By iteratively applying Lemma
G.2
, we have the coefficient of
(x-x_{0})^{\gamma}
in
\widehat{f}(x)^{k}
can be bounded by

{|\gamma|\choose\gamma}\frac{(2^{|\gamma|})^{k}\cdot C_{f}^{k}}{|\gamma|!}.

Therefore, when taking summation over
k
, the coefficient of
(x-x_{0})^{\gamma}
in
\widehat{g}(\widehat{f}(x))
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{k\leq d_{g}}\frac{1}{k!}{|\gamma|\choose\gamma}\frac{(2^{|\gamma|})^{k}\cdot C_{f}^{k}}{|\gamma|!}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{|\gamma|\choose\gamma}\frac{1}{|\gamma|!}\cdot{\exp(2^{|\gamma|}C_{f})}.

∎

proof of Lemma
G.4
.

Note that by the binomial theorem, we have

g(u,v)=\sum_{|\gamma|\leq d_{f}}{|\gamma|\choose\gamma}\frac{\partial^{\gamma}f(x_{0})}{|\gamma|!}\sum_{\alpha+\beta=\gamma}{\gamma\choose\alpha\quad\beta}(u-u_{0})^{\alpha}(v-v_{0})^{\beta},

which is the same as the formula stated in the Lemma.
∎

proof of Lemma
G.5
.

Denote
B=\sup_{u\in U}\|u-u_{0}\|_{\infty}
, and
U^{\prime}=\{u:\|u-u_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq B\}
.

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{U}(u-u_{0})^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}u\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{U^{\prime}}\Big|(u-u_{0})^{\alpha}\Big|\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i}\int_{-B\leq u_{i}\leq B}|\Delta u_{i}|^{\alpha_{i}}\mathrm{d}\Delta u_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{i}\frac{2B^{\alpha_{i}+1}}{\alpha_{i}+1}.

Therefore, the coefficient of the
(v-v_{0})^{\beta}
term is bounded by:

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha:|(\alpha,\beta)|\leq d_{g}}{|\alpha,\beta|\choose\alpha,\beta}\frac{|\partial^{(\alpha,\beta)}g(u_{0},v_{0})|}{|\alpha,\beta|!}\Big|\int_{u\in U}(u-u_{0})^{\alpha}\mathrm{d}u\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}{|\alpha,\beta|\choose\alpha,\beta}\frac{C_{g,\beta}}{|\alpha,\beta|!}\prod_{i}\frac{2B^{\alpha_{i}+1}}{\alpha_{i}+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{g,\beta}}{\prod_{i}\beta_{i}!}\sum_{\alpha}\prod_{i}\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha_{i}!}\cdot\frac{2B^{\alpha_{i}+1}}{\alpha_{i}+1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{g,\beta}}{\prod_{i}\beta_{i}!}\prod_{i}\sum_{\alpha_{i}}\frac{2B^{\alpha_{i}+1}}{(\alpha_{i}+1)!}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{g,\beta}}{\prod_{i}\beta_{i}!}2^{d}\exp(Bd).

∎
